L48: ## 1 Executive Summary
L49: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L50: Established Name:	Asciminib
Trade Name:	Scemblix
Also Known As:	ABL-001
Therapeutic Class:	Kinase inhibitor
Chemical Class:	Small molecule
Pharmacologic Class:	ABL/BCR-ABL1 tyrosine kinase inhibitor
Mechanism of Action:	Inhibition of tyrosine kinase by binding to the myristoyl pocket of BCR-ABL1
L51: Asciminib is an orally administered ABL/BCR-ABL1 tyrosine kinase inhibitor. Unlike other BCR-ABL TKIs that bind to the ATP site of BCR-ABL1 protein, asciminib binds to the myristoyl pocket of BCR-ABL1 protein and mimics myristoylated Gly-2 residue and stabilizes inactive conformation of the enzyme. Asciminib is currently approved for the treatment of adult patients with:
L52: - Philadelphia chromosome-positive chronic myeloid leukemia (Ph+ CML) in chronic phase (CP), previously treated with two or more tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs).
- Ph+ CML in CP with the T315I mutation.
L53: The Applicant now submits a supplementary New Drug Application (sNDA) to support an indication for the treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed or previously treated Philadelphia chromosome-positive chronic myeloid leukemia (Ph+ CML) in chronic phase (CP).
L54: The Applicant submits an sNDA with results from randomized phase 3 study CABL001J12301 (ASC4FIRST) to support the indication of newly diagnosed CML-CP based on major molecular response (MMR) at 48 weeks. The trial to support the indication of previously treated CML-CP is a single-arm study CABL001AUS08 in patients treated with asciminib as second line therapy and supported by the approved indication for the treatment as third line or higher therapy.
L55: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L56: For the treatment of patients with newly diagnosed chronic myeloid leukemia (Ph+ CML) in chronic phase (CP), Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with one adequate and well-controlled clinical investigation and confirmatory evidence.
L57: For the treatment of patients with relapsed or intolerant Ph+ CML in CP, Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with two or more adequate and well-controlled clinical investigations.
L58: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L59: Information submitted by the Applicant provides substantial evidence for the effectiveness of asciminib for the treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed or previously treated Ph+ CML in CP. For the indication of the treatment of newly diagnosed CML-CP, the efficacy of asciminib has been established primarily on the results of a single randomized, open-label, active-controlled, multicenter trial in patients with newly diagnosed CML-CP treated with asciminib versus investigator selected TKI (Study CABL001J12301, ASC4FIRST, referred to as study J12301). The recommendation is based on the finding of improved major molecular response (MMR) at 48 weeks in study J12301 to support accelerated approval for an endpoint likely to predict longer term disease control and survival. The current framework for regular approval for a newly diagnosed CML-CP indication requires 5 years of efficacy follow up. Support of the efficacy of asciminib for the newly diagnosed indication was provided by the established activity of asciminib in later lines of therapy for the treatment of CML-CP for which it is already approved.
L60: In study J12301, 405 patients were randomized (1:1) to asciminib or investigator selected tyrosine kinase inhibitors (IS-TKI including imatinib, dasatinib, nilotinib, or bosutinib, selected prior to randomization). Patients were stratified according to EUTOS long-term survival (ELTS) risk group (low, intermediate, high), and pre-randomization selection of TKI (imatinib or 2G TKIs stratum composed of dasatinib, nilotinib, and bosutinib). Patients could receive a TKI for up to 2 weeks prior to randomization for disease control. Patients continued treatment until unacceptable toxicity or treatment failure occurred.
L61: The interim analysis of J12301 included a prespecified assessment of MMR at 48 weeks in all patients and in the imatinib stratum. MMR at 96 weeks in all patients and in the imatinib stratum are key secondary endpoints and were not available at the time of this analysis. Other secondary endpoints include MMR at other scheduled time points, MMR by all scheduled time points, time to response, duration of response, time to treatment failure, progression free survival, and overall survival.
L62: At the time of analysis, MMR (defined as BCR-ABL1 ratio \le 0.1\% on the International Scale) at 48 weeks was significantly better on the asciminib arm at 68% (95% CI: 61, 74) compared to that in the IS-TKI arm at 49% (95% CI: 42, 56). The treatment difference in MMR rate at 48 weeks was 19% (95% CI: 10, 28; two-sided p value of &lt;0.001, stratified by the pre-randomization TKI and ELTS risk group). In the imatinib stratum, MMR at 48 weeks was significantly better on the asciminib arm at 69% (95% CI: 59, 78) compared to that in the imatinib stratum at 40% (95% CI: 31, 50). The treatment difference in MMR rate at 48 weeks was 30% (95% CI: 17, 42; two-sided p value of &lt;0.001, stratified by ELTS risk group). MMR rates at 48 weeks in patients receiving asciminib and IS-TKIs within the other TKIs stratum were 66% (95% CI: 56%, 75%) and 58% (95% CI: 48%, 68%), respectively. This comparison was not statistically tested.
L63: Median time to MMR in patients receiving asciminib was 24 weeks (95% CI: 24 to 25 weeks) and in patients receiving IS-TKIs was 36 weeks (95% CI: 36 to 49 weeks). Exploratory subgroup
L64: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L65: analyses were performed over demographics and baseline disease characteristics. Analyses over subgroups showed a consistent treatment effect favoring asciminib.
L66: The review team recommends accelerated approval for asciminib for the treatment of newly diagnosed Ph+ CML in CP.
L67: For the indication of the treatment of adult patients with previously treated CML-CP, the efficacy of asciminib has been established based on the results of a two randomized, open-label, active-controlled trials: (1) in patients previously treated with two or more TKIs with asciminib versus bosutinib (Study CABL001A2301, ASCEMBL) which led to the approval in third line and (2) in patients with newly diagnosed CML-CP with asciminib versus IS-TKI (study CABL001J12301, ASC4FIRST) submitted to support the frontline indication as reviewed herein. The expansion of the indication to second line was supported by study CABL001AUS08 (referred to as study AUS08), a single arm study of asciminib treatment in patients treated as second line (2L) therapy, and extrapolation by PK/PD modeling.
L68: The interim analysis of AUS08 included a prespecified assessment of MMR at 6 months in patients evaluable for efficacy at that timepoint. At the interim analysis, 71 patients were enrolled in the 2L cohort, and 28 patients were evaluable for efficacy at 6 months having completed the efficacy assessment at that timepoint or discontinued prior to the timepoint. The MMR rate at 6 months was 43% (12/28; 95% CI: 24, 63). For patients who discontinued their prior TKI due to lack of efficacy, the MMR rate at 6 months was 31% (5/16; 95% CI: 11, 59). For patients who discontinued their prior TKI due to lack of tolerability, the MMR rate at 6 months was 58% (7/12; 95% CI: 28, 85). In the patients treated with asciminib as 2L treatment in study AUS08, the MMR rate at 6 months was intermediate to the MMR rate observed in the 1L cohort in study J12301 and the 3L+ cohort in study A2301, as generally expected for this population. The results of AUS08 support activity of asciminib in previously treated CML-CP. PK/PD modeling was also used to support extrapolation for the previously treated CML-CP indication.
L69: The review team recommends regular approval for asciminib for the treatment of previously treated Ph+ CML in CP.
L70: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L71: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L72: Chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) is a rare myeloproliferative neoplasm characterized by the Philadelphia (Ph) chromosome found in up to 95% of patients. The Ph chromosome results from a reciprocal translocation t(9;22) leading to the chimeric BCR::ABL1 fusion oncogene. Patients with newly diagnosed CML-CP may be treated with imatinib, dasatinib, nilotinib, and bosutinib based on risk scores, preexisting comorbidities and concomitant medications of the patients. Asciminib is an orally administered ABL/BCR-ABL1 tyrosine kinase inhibitor that binds to the myristoyl pocket of BCR-ABL1 protein, currently approved for the treatment of patients with CML in chronic phase previously treated with two or more TKIs.
L73: The recommendation for accelerated approval for asciminib for the treatment of newly diagnosed Ph+ CML in CP is based on the results of a randomized controlled trial evaluating asciminib versus investigator selected TKI (Study CABL001J12301, ASC4FIRST, referred to as study J12301). Supportive efficacy and safety information in this population was provided by the established activity of asciminib in later lines of therapy for the treatment of CML-CP for which it is already approved.
L74: In study J12301, 405 patients were randomized (1:1) to asciminib or investigator selected tyrosine kinase inhibitors (IS-TKI including imatinib, dasatinib, nilotinib, or bosutinib, selected prior to randomization). Patients were stratified according to EUTOS long-term survival (ELTS) risk group (low, intermediate, high), and pre-randomization selection of TKI (imatinib or 2G TKIs stratum composed of dasatinib, nilotinib, and bosutinib). The interim analysis of J12301 included a prespecified assessment of MMR at 48 weeks in all patients and in the imatinib stratum. MMR at 96 weeks in all patients and in the imatinib stratum are key secondary endpoints. At the time of analysis, MMR (defined as BCR-ABL1 ratio \le 0.1\% on the International Scale) at 48 weeks was significantly better on the asciminib arm at 68% (95% CI: 61, 74) compared to that in the IS-TKI arm at 49% (95% CI: 42, 56). The treatment difference in MMR rate at 48 weeks was 19% (95% CI: 10, 28; two-sided p value of &lt;0.001, stratified by the pre-randomization TKI and ELTS risk group). In the imatinib stratum, MMR at 48 weeks was significantly better on the asciminib arm at 69% (95% CI: 59, 78) compared to that in the imatinib stratum at 40% (95% CI: 31, 50). The treatment difference in MMR rate at 48 weeks was 30% (95% CI: 17, 42; two-sided p value of &lt;0.001, stratified by ELTS risk group). MMR rates at 48 weeks in patients receiving asciminib and IS-TKIs within the other TKIs stratum were 66% (95% CI: 56%, 75%) and 58% (95% CI: 48%, 68%), respectively. This comparison was not statistically tested.
L75: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L76: In the randomized study, patients with newly diagnosed CML in CP were treated with either asciminib or IS-TKI. The exposure in the asciminib arm was longer than the IS-TKI arm due to more discontinuations in the IS-TKI arm due to lack of efficacy or dose modifications for toxicity. The overall rate of serious adverse events was lower in the asciminib arm, though the safety profile differed between the arms and should be considered when choosing the appropriate treatment for a patient based on underlying comorbidities. Serious adverse reactions occurred in 11% of patients who received asciminib. Serious adverse reactions in \ge 1\% included pancreatitis (1%), and musculoskeletal pain (1%). In 200 patients, the most common (\ge 20\%) adverse reaction excluding laboratory abnormalities in patients who received asciminib was musculoskeletal pain. The most common select laboratory abnormalities that worsened from baseline in \ge 20\% of patients who received asciminib were lymphocyte count decreased, leukocyte count decreased, platelet count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, calcium corrected decreased, lipase increased, cholesterol increased, uric acid increased, alanine aminotransferase (ALT) increased, alkaline phosphatase (ALP) increased, hemoglobin decreased, and triglycerides increased.
L77: The FDA approach toward approval in CML in CP the newly diagnosed population is to grant accelerated approval when at least 48 weeks of efficacy and safety data is available. For conversion to regular approval, the FDA requires 5 years of efficacy and safety information. Follow up to the later timepoint is required only from the study-drug treatment arm in the case of an active-controlled study to demonstrate benefit with use of the study drug. In this submission, the Applicant provided efficacy and safety information from study J12301 at 48 weeks. The key secondary endpoints of the study are MMR at 96 weeks in all treated patients and in the imatinib stratum. The study will continue for at least 5 years. Therefore, the data from J12301 supports accelerated approval in patients with newly diagnosed CML in CP. A PMR will be issued to obtain an interim study report at 96 weeks and results with at least 5 years of follow up.
L78: The recommendation for regular approval for patients with previously treated CML in CP is based on the established efficacy in patients treated with two or more prior TKIs and supported by patients treated with one prior TKI in study CABL001AUS08 (referred to as study AUS08), a single arm study of asciminib treatment in patients treated as first or second line (2L) therapy, and extrapolation by PK/PD modeling.
L79: The interim analysis of AUS08 included a prespecified assessment of MMR at 6 months in patients evaluable for efficacy at that timepoint. At the interim analysis, 71 patients were enrolled in the 2L cohort, and 28 patients were evaluable for efficacy at 6 months having completed the efficacy assessment at that timepoint or discontinued prior to the timepoint. The MMR rate at 6 months was 43% (12/28; 95% CI: 24, 63). In the patients treated with asciminib as 2L treatment in study AUS08, the MMR rate at 6 months was intermediate to the MMR rate observed in the 1L cohort in study J12301 and the 3L+ cohort in study A2301, as generally expected for this population. Safety in 71 patients treated with asciminib as second line therapy was generally consistent with that observed in other lines of therapy.
L80: The durability of MMR responses and safety in this population supports regular approval for the treatment of patients with previously treated
L81: ## CML in CP.
L82: The safety of asciminib was further described in relevant warnings and precautions, with the occurrence of specified adverse reactions evaluated in the overall safety population from three studies treated at doses of asciminib as monotherapy from 10 mg to 200 mg twice daily. Warnings and precautions include myelosuppression, pancreatic toxicity, hypertension, hypersensitivity, cardiovascular toxicity, and embryofetal toxicity.
L83: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	CML is a clonal myeloproliferative disease driven by the BCR-ABL1 fusion product from the translocation of t(9; 22) known as the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph).
The overall life expectancy in patients with CML in CP is approaching normal with treatment; however, patients with resistance or intolerance to available therapies are at higher risk of progressive disease and have worse prognosis.
In the US, it is estimated that approximately 9280 new cases of CML will be diagnosed and about 1,280 people will die of the malignancy during 2023
CML in chronic phase is a rare, but serious and life-threatening disease.
Current Treatment Options	Five other BCR-ABL TKIs are approved for the treatment of CML in CP (imatinib, dasatinib, nilotinib, bosutinib, and ponatinib).
Patients with newly diagnosed CML-CP may be treated with imatinib, dasatinib, nilotinib, and bosutinib.
First-line treatment should be based on risk scores, preexisting comorbidities and concomitant medications of the patients.
Treatment of relapsed CMP-CP is limited to second generation BCR-ABL TKIs (dasatinib, nilotinib, and bosutinib)
Ponatinib is approved for patients with CML-CP previously treated
There is a need for additional effective therapies for patients with CML in CP in newly diagnosed patients and those previously treated.

Benefit	Study CABL001J12301 (ASC4FIRST) was a randomized (1:1), open-label, active controlled trial of asciminib monotherapy versus IS-TKI, stratified by ELTS risk score and pre-randomization TKI selection (imatinib or 2G TKI). The primary endpoints were MMR at 48 weeks in all patients and in the imatinib stratum. MMR at 48 weeks was 68% (95% CI: 61, 74) in the asciminib arm compared 49% (95% CI: 42, 56) in the IS-TKI arm with a treatment difference of 19% (95% CI: 10, 28 and two-sided p value of <0.001, stratified. In the imatinib stratum, MMR at 48 weeks was 69% (95% CI: 59, 78) in the asciminib arm compared to 40% (95% CI: 31, 50) in the imatinib arm with a treatment difference of 30% (95% CI: 17, 42; two-sided p value of <0.001, stratified. Median time to MMR in patients receiving asciminib was 24 weeks (95% CI: 24 to 25 weeks) and in patients receiving IS-TKIs was 36 weeks (95% CI: 36 to 49 weeks).
Study CABL001AUS08 was a non-randomized, open-label, dose escalation, study of asciminib monotherapy in patients with CML in CP previously treated with one prior TKI. The efficacy population included 28 patients who were evaluable for efficacy at 6 months or discontinued prior to 6 months. The efficacy endpoints of the study included molecular responses at scheduled timepoints. The MMR
The endpoint of MMR at 48 weeks in all patients and in the imatinib stratum support effectiveness in patients with newly diagnosed CML-CP.
Asciminib shows a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in MMR at 48 weeks compared to IS-TKI and in the imatinib stratum in this population. The magnitude and duration of major molecular response are clinically meaningful.
The endpoint of MMR at 6 months in patients with one prior TKI treatment with the MMR at 96 weeks in patients with two or more prior TKI treatment supports the effectiveness of asciminib for previously treated CML-CP.

Risk and Risk Management	In the randomized study of patients with newly diagnosed CML in CP, patients were treated with 80 mg daily asciminib or IS-TKI at the approved dose.
In 200 patients, the most common (\ge 20\%) adverse reaction in patients who received asciminib was musculoskeletal pain.
Serious adverse reactions occurred in 11% of patients who received asciminib. Serious adverse reactions in \ge 1\% included pancreatitis (1%), and musculoskeletal pain (1%). No deaths occurred within 30 days after the last dose of the asciminib.
Safety in 71 patients treated with asciminib as second line therapy was generally consistent with that observed in other lines of therapy.
In the pooled safety population of 556 patients, the most common (\ge 20\%) adverse reactions in patients who received SCEMBLIX were musculoskeletal pain, rash, fatigue, upper respiratory tract infection, headache, abdominal pain, and diarrhea.
Common select laboratory abnormalities include myelosuppression (decreased lymphocyte count, leukocyte count, platelet count, neutrophil count, and hemoglobin), elevated pancreatic enzymes (lipase and amylase increased), cholesterol increased, and elevated liver enzymes (ALT increased, alkaline phosphatase increased, and AST increased), triglycerides increased, and creatine kinase increased.
The safety profile of asciminib treatment at 80 mg daily for patients with newly diagnosed CML in CP is tolerable and manageable. Safety observed in patients with one prior TKI treatment is consistent with that observed in later lines of treatment.
Long-term safety information is needed in this population since asciminib is intended for prolonged use and there may be potential for cumulative toxicity.
Risks overall are sufficiently addressed through warnings and precautions in the United States Prescribing Information.

Warnings and precautions include myelosuppression, pancreatic toxicity, hypertension, hypersensitivity, cardiovascular toxicity, and embryo-fetal toxicity, and were updated to include the pooled safety population.

L94: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L95: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L96: ## The Applicant's Position:
L97: Chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) is a clonal myeloproliferative disorder of transformed, hematopoietic progenitor cells characterized by overproduction of immature myeloid cells and mature granulocytes in the spleen, bone marrow, and peripheral blood. The hallmark of CML is the Philadelphia (Ph) chromosome found in up to 95% of patients (Seong et al 1999). The Ph chromosome results from a reciprocal translocation t(9;22)(q34;q11) which creates a chimeric protein (BCR::ABL1) promoting aberrant cell growth and replication through downstream signaling pathways such as RAS, RAF, JUN kinase, MYC, and STAT (Jabbour, Kantarjian 2022).
L98: With a constant worldwide incidence of 1-2 new cases/100,000 per year, the prevalence of CML is steadily increasing due to improved long-term outcomes with tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI) treatment (Senapati et al 2023) (Jabbour, Kantarjian 2022, ELN 2020). CML mainly affects adult patients with a median age at diagnosis of 57 to 66 years. In the US, it is estimated that approximately 9280 new cases of CML will be diagnosed and about 1,280 people will die of the malignancy during 2023 (SEER 2024, NCCN 2024).
L99: Clinically, most patients are diagnosed in the chronic phase (CP), characterized by anemia, splenomegaly, and leukocytosis with generally few constitutional symptoms like fatigue, weight loss, malaise, early satiety, and abdominal fullness. Without treatment for around 20% of the patients, the disease may transition into blast phase (BP) (Faderl et al 1999).
L100: The response to treatment in CML is monitored based on hematological, cytogenetic, and mainly molecular levels of *BCR::ABL1* by RQ-PCR test. Molecular responses expressed in % (standardized to an international scale (IS) as the ratio of *BCR::ABL1* transcripts to *ABL1* transcripts) provide an objective measure of efficacy for guiding treatment and monitoring patients with CML (Press 2010, Cross et al 2012, Greiner et al 2020). A *BCR::ABL1* IS level of &gt; 1% at 12 months is considered a failure of treatment, whereas a *BCR::ABL1* IS level of ≤ 0.1% (major molecular response; MMR) at 12 months is considered an optimal response to treatment (ELN 2020, NCCN 2024).
L101: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L102: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment about the definition, incidence, clinical features of CML, and that response is monitored based on mainly molecular levels of BCR/ABL1 by RQ-PCR. Achievement of major molecular response (MMR) defined as BCR/ABL1 ≤ 0.1% at 12 months is considered optimal response to treatment.

L113: ## The Applicant's Position:
L114: The introduction of the TKI imatinib in 2001, the first drug targeting an oncogenic mutation, revolutionized the treatment of patients with CML. This was followed by the approval of several second-generation (2G) adenosine triphosphate (ATP)-binding site TKIs including dasatinib, nilotinib, bosutinib (Sweet et al 2023).
L115: TKI in current use: in routine medical practice, these ATP-binding TKIs are used interchangeably in all lines of treatment of CML-CP irrespective of the approved line of treatment. Although TKI treatment has improved the survival rates for patients with CML, and patients with optimal treatment response may expect near-normal life expectancy (García-Gutiérrez, Hernández-
L116: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L117: Boluda 2019 and Jabbour, Kantarjian 2022), the requirement of chronic therapy for CML has underlined the importance of treatment selection for newly-diagnosed patients. The primary aims of treatment in this population are to achieve early optimal responses to prevent disease progression, maintain quality of life, sometimes to achieve TFR in selected patients, and reduce the likelihood of serious and irreversible side effects. It is also crucial to choose the appropriate TKI option for second-line (2L) treatment for those who develop resistance or intolerance to 1L treatment with similar considerations (Cortes, Lang 2021).
L118: The personalized approach to selection of treatment for CML is reflected in international CML treatment guidelines (ELN 2020, NCCN 2024). There is currently no single standard of care for patients with newly diagnosed CML-CP (Table 1). Approximately 50% of newly-diagnosed patients in clinical practice are treated with imatinib and the remaining 50% with one of the 2G TKIs (internal market research conducted via (b) (4) and IQVIA in the US and EU in June 2023; Campbell et al 2019).
L119: The selection of the 1L treatment option is based on individual patient need and preference, disease characteristics (Sokal, Hasford, EUTOS or ELTS), pre-existing comorbidities, and concomitant medications, as well as the individual treatment goal (Saglio, Jabbour 2018, NCCN 2024, ELN 2020). In elderly patients or newly-diagnosed patients with low-risk score, imatinib is more frequently selected. From an efficacy perspective, imatinib is less potent than 2G TKIs, delivering lower cytogenetic and molecular response rates (e.g. MMR rates at 12 months of 22% to 37%) in many comparative studies against 2G TKIs (Saglio et al 2010, Kantarjian et al 2010, Hochhaus et al 2016, Cortes, Kantarjian 2016, Cortes 2018). The 2G TKIs are more frequently used in younger patients, in patients with high-risk score, and in an attempt to attain TFR. However, the use of 2G TKIs is often limited by their serious, and sometimes irreversible, adverse events, including severe organ dysfunction (Hochhaus et al 2016, ELN 2020, NCCN 2024). Similar to that in 1L, selection of the TKI to be used in 2L also needs to be based on a number of considerations including patient and disease characteristics, comorbidities and prior therapy safety profile (history of intolerance and/or treatment related complications with 1L treatment). For patients treated with imatinib in 1L a 2G TKI is used in 2L, while for patients treated with a 2G TKI in 1L an alternate 2G TKI is recommended (NCCN 2024). However, on exhaustion of 2G TKI options or in case of comorbidities precluding the use of 2G TKIs, imatinib use in later lines is also seen in medical practice (Mauro et al 2023). In patients with no BCR::ABL1 kinase domain mutations, there is no clear recommendation for any particular TKI in 2L. Treatment sequencing in CML is very much personalized to the patient, and either imatinib or any of the 2G TKIs are used irrespective of line of treatment.
L120: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L121: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that patients with newly diagnosed chronic phase (CP)-CML are treated with imatinib or one of the second-generation tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs) (dasatinib, nilotinib, or bosutinib). First-line (1L) treatment should be based on risk scores (Sokal, Hasford, EUTOS or ELTS), preexisting comorbidities and concomitant medications of the
L122: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L123: patients. The treatment of choice is based on similar criteria in second-line (2L) treatment.
L124: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of safety and efficacy of currently approved TKIs for patients with newly diagnosed CP-CML based on the prescribing information for the approved TKIs and the published literature.
L125: ## 3 Regulatory Background
L126: ## 3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L127: ## The Applicant's Position:
L128: Asciminib (Scemblix®, ABL001) was initially approved on 29-Oct-2021 for the treatment of adult patients with Ph+ CML in CP, previously treated with two or more TKIs under accelerated approval, and for the treatment of adult patients with Ph+ CML in CP harboring the T315I mutation under regular approval. Regular approval for the treatment of adult patients with Ph+ CML-CP, previously treated with two or more TKIs was subsequently granted on 22-Oct-2022.
L129: The purpose of this current submission is to seek approval for asciminib for the treatment of adult patients with newly-diagnosed or previously-treated Ph+ CML-CP.
L130: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L131: FDA confirms the Applicant's assessment. The current approval for asciminib is for CML-CP previously treated with two or more tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs), known as third line (3L) treatment. The proposed indication for the treatment of patients with newly diagnosed or previously treated CML-CP would include second line (2L) treatment.

L142: ## 4 Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L143: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L144: OSI inspection was requested for the Applicant and for clinical sites. In Study CABL001J12301 (briefly referred to as J12301) 8 (4%) patients in the asciminib arm and 10 (4.9%) patients in the investigator-selected TKI (IS-TKI) arm were enrolled in the US. Inspection of 2 international clinical sites was requested because there was insufficient domestic data and most experience with asciminib has been at ex-US sites. Therefore, it was planned to include ex-US sites in the OSI inspections to verify the quality of conduct of the study. OSI inspected two foreign clinical investigators (one in Canada, site #1002; one in Czech Republic, site #2080) and the Applicant. These sites were chosen because they contained sufficient numbers of patients in safety and efficacy populations, and they had outliers in safety and efficacy outcomes. OSI review team concluded that study CABL001J12301 appeared to have been conducted adequately and the study data from the inspected clinical investigator sites were considered acceptable.
L145: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L146: The Applicant did not submit any new product quality information. Asciminib drug product is provided as film-coated tablets containing 20 mg, 40 mg, or 100 mg of asciminib per tablet.
L147: ## 4.3. Clinical Microbiology
L148: Not applicable.
L149: ## 4.4. Devices and Companion Diagnostic Issues
L150: Not applicable. Response was monitored based on molecular levels of BCR/ABL1 by RQ-PCR at an Applicant-designated laboratory.

L165: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L166: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L167: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L168: SCEMBLIX (asciminib) is a kinase inhibitor that is currently approved for the treatment of adult patients with Philadelphia chromosome-positive chronic myeloid leukemia (Ph+ CML) in chronic phase (CP), previously treated with two or more tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs), and Ph+ CML in CP with the T315I mutation. The recommended dosages are 80 mg once daily (QD) or 40 mg twice daily (BID) for patients with Ph+ CML-CP and 200 mg BID for patients with Ph+ CML in CP harboring the T315I mutation. Asciminib should be taken orally and avoid food for at least 2 hours before and 1 hour after taking SCEMBLIX.
L169: The Applicant submitted this efficacy supplement (S008) to support the application for the use of asciminib monotherapy in the treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed or previously treated Ph+ CML-CP. Specifically, the Applicant is seeking the approval of an indication in a broad population of newly diagnosed or previously treated patients, regardless of the number of prior TKIs, including those in second line (2L) of treatment.
L170: The clinical pharmacology sections of this supplement were supported by PK data from patients with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP from Study J12301, population PK and exposure-response analyses, and PK-pharmacogenomic analyses. The clinical pharmacology review focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosages of 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID for the first line (1L) indication and the PK/PD modeling to support extrapolation to the 2L indication. The potential for genetic variants in UGT2B7 and UGT2B17 to impact the PK of asciminib was also evaluated.
L171: ## **Recommendations:**
L172: From the Office of Clinical Pharmacology (OCP) perspective, this efficacy supplement is approvable and there are no clinical pharmacology-related postmarketing requirements (PMR) or postmarketing commitments (PMC). The key review issues and recommendations are summarized below.
L173: Review issues	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from:Newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP: Study J12301 demonstrated statistically significant meaningful improvements in major molecular response (MMR)
L174: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L175: at 48 weeks for asciminib 80 mg QD vs. investigator-selected TKIs in patients with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP.
2L Ph+ CML-CP:Study CABL001AUS08 (AUS08), ongoing, Phase 2, single arm dose escalation study of asciminib in patients with 2L Ph+ CP-CML. In the 14 patients with 6-month follow-up, MMR was observed in 8/14 patients (57.1%) administered asciminib 80 mg QD.
Supportive PK/PD modeling: PK/PD modeling was used to support extrapolation to the 2L setting based on asciminib 1L and 3L+ data as well as borrowing the difference between the 1L and 2L effect on the model parameters from the nilotinib exposure-efficacy analysis and applying the differences to simulate the time-course of BCR::ABL1 transcript levels. The predicted MMR rates of asciminib 80 mg QD or 40 mg BID in the 2L setting ranged from 54 – 63% at Week 48 and 66 – 74% at Week 96, which fell within MMR rates for the 1L and 3L settings and were generally consistent with the observed MMR of 57% at the 6-month follow-up.


General dosing instructions	
The proposed dosages of 40 mg BID or 80 mg QD are acceptable for patients with newly diagnosed or previously treated Ph+ CML-CP per the following rationale:
The observed efficacy/safety results from pivotal Study J12301 supports 80 mg QD in newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP.
The observed efficacy/safety results from supportive Study AUS08 supports 80 mg QD in 2L Ph+ CMLCP.
Original approval for the indication of previously treated with two or more TKIs in patients with Ph+ CML-CP based on data from the pivotal Study A2301.
L176: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L177: The recommended dosages are 80 mg QD or 40 mg BID.
Population PK simulation shows similar PK exposures between 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID dosing regimens. Similar efficacy is expected for 40 mg BID (e.g., similar AUC, lower Cmax and higher Cmin).
The supportive PK/PD modeling predicted MMR rates at 48 and 96 weeks that fell within those for the 1L (Study J12301) and 3L (Study A2301) settings and were generally consistent with the observed preliminary MMR from Study AUS08 in the 2L setting.

Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	
The proposed dosing is the same as the current recommendations in the approved USPI. Exploratory analysis suggests that polymorphic variants in UGT2B7 and UGT2B17 do not have a clinically significant impact on the PK of asciminib and no dosage adjustment is needed.

Labeling	
There are no additional updates in clinical pharmacology sections.

Bridge between the to-be-marketed and clinical trial formulations	
Not applicable.
L178: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L179: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L180: ## The Applicant's Position:
L181: An integrated assessment of PK data from clinical studies, dedicated clinical pharmacology studies in healthy subjects and studies in participants with Ph+ CML-CP along with the results of pharmacometric analyses providing in-depth interpretation of PK and PD data of asciminib were presented in the original SCP. The original SCP consisted of basic PK properties of asciminib, PBPK modeling, population PK (PopPK) analyses (using data from participants with Ph+ CML-CP/AP in Study X2101 and participants with 3L+ Ph+ CML-CP in Study A2301), and exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety relationships including exposure-QTc relationships in participants with CML.
L182: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L183: Additional asciminib PK data in participants with Ph+ CML-CP from Study J12301 are provided in the current submission. Furthermore, the PopPK analyses were updated to include data from participants with CML-BP and Ph+ ALL in Study X2101, as well as data from 3 additional studies: Study J12301, the Phase I hepatic impairment study CABL001A2103, and the Phase I renal impairment study CABL001A2105. Exposure-response analyses including pooled data of participants with CML from Studies J12301, A2301, and X2101 were also provided.
L184: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L185: The PK data from patients with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP from Study J12301 were generally consistent with previous characterization of the pharmacology and clinical pharmacokinetics of asciminib. The updated population PK modeling in the current submission did not identify any additional significant covariates on the PK of asciminib. See Appendix 19.4.2 for details.
L186: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L187: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L188: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L189: The recommended dose of asciminib is a total daily dose of 80 mg, taken either as 80 mg QD or 40 mg BID which was approved by FDA in patients with Ph+ CML-CP, previously treated with two or more TKIs (Scemblix USPI 2021). The proposed dose of 80 mg QD for participants with newly-diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP in Study J12301 was based on clinical experience in participants with Ph+ CML-CP in Studies X2101 (multiple doses including 80 mg QD) and A2301 (40 mg BID, equivalent to a total daily dose of 80 mg) and the PK/PD modeling based exposure-response and exposure-safety analyses [CO-Section 4.1].
L190: Data from Study J12301 confirmed that the benefit/risk profile of 80 mg QD in 1L is not different from that of 40 mg BID assessed in 3L+ in Study A2301. In addition, the updated PK/PD modeling further confirmed that the benefit/risk for 80 mg QD is comparable to that seen with the 40 mg BID dose, and hence asciminib 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID can be used interchangeably [CO-Section 4.1].
L191: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L192: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosages of 40 mg BID or 80 mg QD for patients with newly diagnosed or previously treated Ph+ CML-CP based on the following rationale:
L193: - Results of pivotal Study J12301 demonstrated favorable benefit/risk profile of asciminib 80 mg QD in patients with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP as compared to investigator-selected TKIs arm and imatinib stratum.
- The observed efficacy/safety results from supportive Study AUS08 supports 80 mg QD in 2L Ph+ CML-CP.
L194: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L195: - Original approval for the indication of previously treated with two or more TKIs in patients with Ph+ CML-CP based on data from Study A2301. The recommended dosages are 80 mg QD or 40 mg BID.
- Population PK simulation shows similar PK exposures between 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID dosing regimens (Table 35). Similar efficacy is expected for 40 mg BID (e.g., similar AUC, lower Cmax and higher Cmin).
- PK/PD modeling was used to support extrapolation to the 2L setting based on asciminib 1L (n=197 from Study J12301) and 3L+ (n=150 from Study A2301) data as well as borrowing the difference between the 1L and 2L effect on the model parameters from the nilotinib exposure-efficacy analysis and applying the differences to simulate the time-course of BCR::ABL1 transcript levels. In summary, the PK/PD model was a 3-compartment model representing quiescent leukemic stem cells (q), proliferating bone marrow cells (p), and resistant cells (r) developed using data from 430 patients with Ph+ CML-CP to describe the time-course of BCR::ABL1 transcript levels. Drug kill of the p cells by asciminib was characterized by an Emax model. The potential influences of the number of previous TKI treatments (i.e., line of treatment) and patient's baseline and disease characteristics (including ELTS risk group) were investigated. The extrapolation of longitudinal BCR::ABL1 transcript levels to asciminib in the 2L setting utilized the difference in the p, r and Emax parameters between nilotinib as first and second line treatments. The predicted MMR rates of asciminib 80 mg QD or 40 mg BID in the 2L setting ranged from 54 – 63% at Week 48 and 66 – 74% at Week 96, which fell within MMR rates for the 1L and 3L settings and were generally consistent with the observed MMR of 57% at the 6-month follow-up (Week 48 MMR not reached yet).
L196: See Appendix 19.4.2 and 19.4.3 for details of the population PK and E-R analyses.
L197: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L198: ## The Applicant's Position:
L199: No therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on demographic factors (body weight, age, gender, race, ethnicity), drug-drug interactions with asciminib as a victim or as a perpetrator, or in special populations (hepatic or renal impairment) [SCP-Section 3.2, Section 3.3, Section 5.1.2].
L200: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L201: The updated population PK modeling in the current submission did not identify any additional significant covariates on the PK of asciminib. There are no additional recommendations for therapeutic individualization of asciminib for the proposed indication.
L202: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L203: ## **6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues**
L204: ## The Applicant's Position:
L205: None
L206: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L207: There are no outstanding issues at this time.
L208: ## **6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review**
L209: ## **6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics**
L210: General characteristics of asciminib PK in terms of bioavailability, absorption, food effect, dose proportionality, distribution, metabolism, and excretion were detailed in the original submission. No new information is provided in support of the current application.
L211: The new PK data of asciminib in Study J12301 is in line with the overall PK profile of asciminib previously reported in the original submission. Data showed that the plasma concentrations of asciminib increased rapidly after oral administration of asciminib 80 mg QD with a median Tmax of 2.0 hr. The geo-mean of peak plasma concentration of asciminib (Cmax) was 1470 ng/mL (CV% geo-mean: 38.2%), geo-mean of AUClast was 8590 ng*hr/mL (CV% geo-mean: 42.2%; note: Tlast was 12 hr post-dose). The trough concentrations (Ctrough) of asciminib were similar throughout the visits (geo-mean range: 160-182 ng/mL) [CO-Section 3.0].
L212: Using the updated final PopPK model, simulated PK metrics for the 40 mg BID and 80 mg QD dosing regimens were obtained. Overall, the simulated mean AUC0-24h values were comparable between the two regimens, while the mean Cmax and Cmin at steady state of 80 mg QD were 1.62-fold and 0.71-fold of that of 40 mg BID, respectively. These simulated metrics based on the updated dataset were consistent with the results of the simulation carried out in the original PopPK analysis (Table 35, [CO-Section 3.0]).
L213: Additionally, based on the new PopPK analysis, no correlation between clearance and number of prior TKIs (irrespective of prior TKI use and/or no prior TKI use) was observed, thus suggesting that asciminib PK is consistent across lines of treatment [CO-Section 3.1.1].
L214: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L215: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the general pharmacology and pharmacokinetic characteristics of asciminib.
L216: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L217: ## **6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions**
L218: ## **6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?**
L219: ## Data
L220: ## The Applicant's Position:
L221: Yes. Results from Studies J12301, A2301, and X2101 provide evidence of positive benefit-risk and support the efficacy claim of asciminib 80 mg total daily dose in adult participants with Ph+ CML-CP. While the clinical pharmacology evaluation does not include a direct assessment of benefit-risk, the exposure-response models provide the following exposure-efficacy and safety relationships.
L222: In participants with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP, a predicted 70% Week-48 MMR rate is achieved based on the participant's dosing history in Study J12301, and an enhanced MMR rate of 75% is predicted in uninterrupted dosing situation. Higher MMR rates are achieved when patients maintained uninterrupted dosing; dose interruptions are predicted to lead to lower MMR rates, highlighting the importance of treatment compliance. The asciminib 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID regimens are predicted to result in comparable MMR rates across treatment lines and timepoints [Exposure-efficacy report-Section 7].
L223: The model-based analysis that characterized the longitudinal BCR::ABL1 by treatment lines (1L, 2L or 3L+) is suitable for extrapolation across lines of asciminib treatment in CML-CP. In the 2L, the total daily dose of 80 mg administered either as once-daily (80 mg QD) or twice-daily (40 mg BID) is predicted to achieve a Week-48 MMR rate of 54-63% and a Week-96 MMR rate of 66-74% [Exposure-efficacy report-Section 7] (Table 38). This predicted MMR rate is in alignment with the preliminary emerging data available from the ongoing Study AUS08 in a cohort of participants with 2L Ph+ CML-CP (Section 8.1.4)
L224: The probability of occurrence of all safety events was lower for newly-diagnosed than for pretreated participants with Ph+ CML. The higher event probability in pretreated participants is expected, given the persistent side effects of prior TKIs, increased incidence of cross-intolerance, and evolution of their disease characteristics including resistant mutations due to prolonged exposure to other TKIs [Exposure-safety report-Section 9].
L225: Over the range of exposure for 1L participants, the probability of grade \ge 3 lipase increase was low and ranged from 5.8-11.2%, indicating a slight positive relationship between asciminib exposure and probability of grade \ge 3 lipase increase. The probability of occurrence of all other safety events is unlikely to increase with increased exposure of asciminib. Comparable exposure was reached with the 40 mg BID and 80 mg QD asciminib doses, and no association was observed between exposure and most safety events, indicating the interchangeability of these two dosing regimens with respect to their safety profile [Exposure-safety report-Section 9].
L226: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L227: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L228: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that the clinical pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of the effectiveness of asciminib in the proposed patient population. Refer to Section 6.2.2.1 General Dosing for details.
L229: ## **6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L230: ## The Applicant's Position:
L231: Yes. In multiple models, evidence of anti-leukemic activity of single-agent asciminib was demonstrated irrespective of dose level (i.e. across total daily doses of 20 to 400 mg) and prior lines of therapy received (Table 38) [PopPK report-Table 5-1].
L232: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L233: FDA agrees with the Applicant that the proposed dosages of 80 mg QD or 40 mg BID are appropriate for the general patient population for which the indications are being sought. Refer to Section 6.2.2.1 General Dosing for details.
L234: ## **6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?**
L235: ## The Applicant's Position:
L236: Based on the assessment of intrinsic factors, no dose adjustment or change in regimen is required based on demographics (age, gender, weight, race, ethnicity), genotypes (UGT2B7 and UGT2B17 genotypes), or in special populations (i.e. hepatic and renal impairment) [SCP-Section 5.1.2].
L237: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L238: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that no additional or different dosage adjustment or management strategy is required based on intrinsic factors.
L239: ## **UGT2B7 and UGT2B17 genotype status and asciminib PK**
L240: Asciminib is mainly metabolized by polymorphic UGTs (UGT2B7: ~28%, UGT2B17: ~16%, UGT1A3/4: ~14%) and CYP3A4 (~35%). The Applicant performed an exploratory study to examine the impact of germline UGT2B7 and UGT2B17 genotype status on the PK of asciminib in blood. The study utilized samples collected during Study J12301. Whole blood for UGT genotyping (PharmacScan gene chip) was collected at baseline from patients in the asciminib arm. Asciminib Cthrough levels were measured in pre-dose samples collected at weeks 2, 4, 12, 24, and 48, and the average Cthrough value for each patient was used for analysis. UGT genotype data from
L241: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L242: 151 patients were available for analysis (149 patients had genotype data for both *UGT2B7* and *UGT2B17* [98.7%], and 2 extra patients had only *UGT2B17* genotype data [100.0%]). C_{\text{trough}} data were available for 145 patients (97.3%) out of the 149 genotyped for *UGT2B7* and *UGT2B17*, and for 147 patients (97.4%) out of the 151 with *UGT2B17* genotype data. *UGT2B7* and *UGT2B17* phenotypes were inferred from genotypes using the PharmacoScan software. Mean asciminib C_{\text{trough}} levels were similar between *UGT2B7* phenotype categories (Kruskal-Wallis test, p-value = 0.064; Figure 1). Likewise, mean asciminib C_{\text{trough}} levels were similar between *UGT2B17* phenotype categories (Kruskal-Wallis test, p-value = 0.762; Figure 2). In conclusion, the results from this exploratory analysis suggest that polymorphic variants in *UGT2B7* and *UGT2B17* do not have a significant clinical impact on the PK of asciminib.
L243: Figure 1 Asciminib mean C_{\text{trough}} by *UGT2B7* phenotype
L244: Source: Applicant's Exploratory UGT Genotype PK Analyses Report, Figure 4-2.
L245: IM: intermediate metabolizer, n = 5. NM: normal metabolizer, n = 57. RM: rapid metabolizer, n = 55. UM: ultrarapid metabolizer, n = 28. Observed *UGT2B7* diplotypes: *1a/*1a (n = 9), *1a/*1b (n = 6), *1a/*1c (n = 6), *1a/*1d (n = 10), *1a/*2a (n = 21), *1a/*2g (n = 1), *1a/*3 (n = 3), *1b/*1b (n = 1), *1b/*1c (n = 1), *1b/*1d (n = 1), *1b/*2a (n = 4), *1b/*3 (n = 3), *1c/*1d (n = 5), *1c/*2a (n = 11), *1d/*1d (n = 4), *1d/*2a (n = 16), *1d/*2g (n = 3), *2a/*2a (n = 28), *2a/*2g (n = 6), *2a/*3 (n = 3), *2g/*2g (n = 1), and *3/*3 (n = 2).

L247: Source: Applicant's Exploratory UGT Genotype PK Analyses Report, Figure 4-4.
L248: IM: intermediate metabolizer, n = 64. NM: normal metabolizer, n = 42. PM: poor metabolizer, n = 41.
L249: Observed *UGT2B17* diplotypes: *1/*1 (n = 41), *1a/*2 (n = 59), *1a/hapB (n = 1), *2/*2 (n = 41), and *2/hapB (n = 5).
L250: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L251: ## The Applicant's Position:
L252: Food consumption decreases asciminib bioavailability, with a high-fat meal having a higher impact on asciminib PK than a low-fat meal. Asciminib AUCinf is decreased by 62.3% with a high-fat meal and by 30% with a low-fat meal compared to the fasted state. Food consumption should be avoided for at least 2 hours before and 1 hour after taking asciminib [SCP-Section 5.1.3.1].
L253: Asciminib as a victim or perpetrator of DDI, both regarding enzymes and transporters, was assessed and described in the original submission. Since the original approval the prescribing information has been updated with new information on DDI. No new information is provided in support of the current application.
L254: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L255: FDA has no additional comments or recommendations for alternative dosing or management strategies based on extrinsic factors.

L270: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L271: which consists of 200 patients from Study CABL001J12301, 156 patients from Study CABL001A2301, and 200 patients from Study CABL001X2101.
L272: The Applicant also provided safety and efficacy information from Study CABL001AUS08 which is an ongoing phase 2, US multi-center, open label, single arm dose escalation study in patients with Philadelphia chromosome positive CML in CP receiving asciminib monotherapy as 2L or 1L treatment. The primary endpoint of this study is MMR rate at 12 months with key efficacy assessments of molecular responses at various timepoints. Six-month interim efficacy results in 28 patients who used asciminib as 2L therapy were evaluated to support the previously treated Philadelphia chromosome positive CML in CP indication. Safety in 71 patients treated in 2L was also evaluated.
L273: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L274: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L275: ## The Applicant's Description:
L276: Efficacy claims are based on the results of the primary analysis of the Phase III registration Study J12301, performed when all randomized participants (n=405) had been on study treatment for at least 48 weeks or had discontinued earlier; this corresponded to a data cut-off date of 28-Nov-2023.
L277: ## 8.1.1. Study J12301
L278: ## Study Design
L279: ## The Applicant's Description:
L280: Study J12301 is an ongoing Phase III, multicenter, open-label, randomized study of oral asciminib 80 mg QD versus IS-TKI in adult participants with newly-diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP, designated to characterize efficacy, safety, and tolerability, and to assess the PK profile of asciminib.
L281: **Study Location:** Conducted across 116 centers (including 16 centers in USA) in 29 countries worldwide.
L282: **Choice of Control Group:** The selection of the TKI in the IS-TKI arm was at the physician's discretion based on disease characteristics, comorbidities and participant preference. The choice of use of any of the currently approved TKIs for newly-diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP in the IS-TKI arm is reflective of the standard of care in clinical practice and minimizes the risk of excluding any group of participants, based on Investigator and/or participant preference, or participant age group, risk category, or specific comorbidities [SCE-Section 1.2.1].
L283: **Diagnostic Criteria:** Per European Leukemia Network (ELN) criteria (Hochhaus et al 2020).
L284: **Dose Selection/Rationale:** Described in Section 6.2.2.1.
L285: **Study Treatments and Treatment Assignment:** Participants were assigned to one of the 2 treatment arms (Arm 1: asciminib 80 mg QD or Arm 2: IS-TKI (imatinib 400 mg QD, nilotinib 300 mg BID, dasatinib 100 mg QD, or bosutinib 400 mg QD) in a 1:1 ratio. Randomization was stratified based on ELTS risk group (low vs. intermediate vs. high) and the PRS-TKI. Enrollment into each stratum i.e imatinib vs. 2G TKI [nilotinib or dasatinib or bosutinib] was controlled at approximately 50% vs. 50%, respectively, to achieve a balance across treatment arms for baseline disease characteristics, prognostic factors, and comorbidities and to reflect standard of care
L286: ## **Scemblix (asciminib)**
L287: treatment used in the study population. No crossover of study treatment and no change of study treatment within the IS-TKI arm was allowed (Figure 3) [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.4].
L288: **Blinding:** Not applicable. An open-label study design was considered appropriate for this study, due to inherent differences in the conditions for drug administration between the treatment arms. Blinding of study treatment would have been very complex and could have increased the likelihood of dosing errors in the study. To minimize the potential impact of the knowledge of treatments, the randomization list was kept strictly confidential. No aggregate statistical analyses by treatment arm were performed prior to database lock; however, an independent data monitoring committee (DMC) reviewed unblinded safety data.
L289: **Dose Modification, Dose Discontinuation:** For participants who did not tolerate the protocol-specified asciminib dosing schedule, dose interruptions and/or reductions were either recommended or mandated to allow participants to continue study treatment. The criteria for dose modification were based on the dose modification guidelines for each toxicity. Dose escalation beyond 80 mg QD for asciminib was not permitted. Dose modifications of the IS-TKI were at the Investigator's discretion and in accordance with institutional practice and local prescribing information [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.4.4].
L290: **Administrative Structure:** Study J12301 was managed by Novartis and supported by:
L291: - A Steering Committee, consisting of selected Investigators and Sponsor representatives, ensuring management of the study in accordance with the protocol.
- An independent DMC, comprising of two physicians with appropriate disease area qualifications and one statistician. The DMC reviewed the unblinded safety data starting approximately 6 months after the first participant had started study treatment. Subsequent reviews were conducted every 4 months until the primary analysis [Study J12301 PA CSR-Appendix 16.1.4].
L292: **Procedures and Schedule:** The efficacy assessments included assessment of molecular, cytogenetic, and hematological response in all randomized participants.
L293: Peripheral blood samples were collected from all participants at screening and every 4 weeks until Week 12 and subsequently every 12 weeks until the end of study treatment for analysis of BCR::ABL1 level via RQ-PCR by a Novartis designated laboratory. Bone marrow aspirates for cytogenetic analyses were performed at screening and upon treatment failure. Hematologic response was assessed by complete blood count and physical examination at each visit [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.5.2].
L294: Safety assessments consisted of collecting all AEs and SAEs, with their severity and relationship to study drug; monitoring of hematology, blood chemistry, coagulation, pregnancy, hepatitis marker specimens; and regular assessments of vital signs, ECOG performance status, ECG; and physical examination (including body weight and height). For all women of childbearing potential, a serum pregnancy test (serum \beta-HCG) was performed as specified in the assessment schedule
L295: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L296: [Study J12301 PA CSR-Appendix 16.1.1-Protocol-Table 8-1] and urine pregnancy tests were to be performed at home every 4 weeks if serum pregnancy test (Serum \beta-HCG) was not performed [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.5.3].
L297: **Concurrent Medications:** In general, concomitant medications and therapies deemed necessary for the supportive care and safety of the participant were allowed, except as specifically prohibited. Concomitant use of hydroxyurea for urgent control of high cell counts during the first 2 weeks of study treatment was allowed. For participants treated with asciminib, the use of bisphosphonates regardless of indication, and anticancer treatments of newly-diagnosed solid cancers (e.g. prostate cancer) that would not impact the level of minimal residual disease of participants was allowed. Use of CYP3A4/5 and CYP2C9 substrates with narrow therapeutic index, strong CYP3A4 inducers, substrates of OATP1B, BCRP or both transporters, and drugs with "Known risk", "Possible risk", or "Conditional risk of Torsades de Pointes" were allowed with caution and with close monitoring to ensure participant safety. For participants treated with IS-TKI, the Investigator was to follow the respective local label for specific directions regarding other drugs that were contra-indicated with the assigned study treatment [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.4.5]
L298: **Treatment Compliance:** was assessed by the Investigator and/or study personnel at each visit using tablet counts (if applicable at the visit), and information provided by the participant. This information was captured in the source document at each visit. All study treatment dispensed and returned was recorded in the Drug Accountability Log. Total daily dose of study treatment administered with start and end date was collected on the dedicated eCRF page. Remote treatment administration compliance was assessed by off-site healthcare professionals, and information provided to the Investigator and/or study personnel [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.4.3.4].
L299: **Rescue Medication:** No rescue medication was allowed.
L300: **Participant Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal:** Participants are planned to continue to receive their assigned treatment until the End of Study, or until premature discontinuation due to treatment failure, disease progression, or intolerance or due to Investigator or participant decision. The End of Study will occur 5 years from the last participant first treatment in the study. Participants who discontinue study treatment prematurely due to any reason are followed up for survival and progression until the End of Study [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.1].

L306: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L307: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L308: Adult participants with newly-diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP (diagnosed within 3 months of enrollment) were eligible for entry. For urgent disease control hydroxyurea and anagrelide or either imatinib or nilotinib, or dasatinib or bosutinib for a period of \le 2 weeks prior to randomization were allowed. Treatment with other anticancer agents including other TKIs prior to randomization was not permitted. No TKI other than the study treatment was to be used post-randomization [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.3].

L311: ## Study Endpoints
L312: ## The Applicant's Description:
L313: The primary and other relevant study objectives, endpoints, and statistical analyses pertaining to the Week 48 analyses are presented in Table 4 [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 8 and Section 9.7].
L314: ## **Robustness and appropriateness of primary efficacy endpoints**
L315: MMR is recognised as the most relevant surrogate of long-term outcome and clinical benefit in patients with Ph+ CML-CP. MMR at 12 months is endorsed as key milestone for achieving optimal response with treatment guidelines (ELN 2020, NCCN 2024) recommending a switch in treatment for most patients not being able to achieve this level of response. Evidence of MMR being a clinically relevant endpoint that is predictive of superior long-term clinical outcomes (PFS, OS), durable long-term cytogenetic remissions, and low rate of disease progression in Ph+ CML-CP has been substantively established (ELN 2020, NCCN 2024, Hughes et al 2010, Hochhaus et al 2017). Additionally, MMR at 12 months is also associated with a higher likelihood of achieving DMR, which is an important criteria for determining TFR eligibility (NCCN 2024). The rapid achievement of DMR indicates the effective depletion of disease burden in patients with CML and association of DMR with lack of disease progression and superior long-term outcomes has been shown (Cortes 2018, Dulucq, Mahon 2016, Branford 2016). Furthermore, sustained DMR is a necessary prerequisite for attempting TFR, which is increasingly selected as a goal of treatment for patients newly diagnosed with CML.

L321: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L322: ## The Applicant's Description:
L323: The planned Statistical Analysis Plan was agreed upon with FDA and finalized prior to conduct of any analysis and is described in Table 4. The primary and supplemental estimands are summarized in [SCE-Section 1.2.1]
L324: ## Analysis sets used were defined as:
L325: - The **Full Analysis Set (FAS)** comprised all participants to whom study treatment was assigned by randomization.
- The **IMA Full Analysis Set (FASIMA)** comprised all participants from the FAS, whose PRS-TKI was imatinib.
- The **2G TKI Full Analysis Set (FAS2GTKI)** comprised all participants from the FAS, whose PRS-TKI was a 2G TKI (nilotinib, dasatinib, or bosutinib).
- For duration of MMR, the **MMR Responder Set, IMA MMR Responder Set, and 2GTKI MMR Responder Set** were used, which comprised the subset of participants from the FAS, FASIMA, or FAS2GTKI, respectively, who achieved MMR at any time.
- For duration of MR4.0, the **MR4.0 Responder Set, IMA MR4.0 Responder Set, and 2G TKI MR4.0 Responder Set** was used, which comprised the subset of participants from the FAS, FASIMA, or FAS2GTKI, respectively, who achieved MR4.0 at any time.
- For duration of MR4.5, the **MR4.5 Responder Set, IMA MR4.5 Responder Set, and 2G TKI MR4.5 Responder Set** was used, which comprised the subset of participants from the FAS, FASIMA, or FAS2GTKI, respectively, who achieved MR4.5 at any time.
- The **Safety Set** comprised of all participants who received at least one dose of any study treatment.
L326: Following the intent-to-treat principle, participants were analyzed according to the treatment arm and stratum they were assigned to at randomization. Efficacy of asciminib was assessed overall compared with all IS-TKIs as well as within each pre-randomization stratum of participants with imatinib (formally tested) and 2G TKIs (not formally tested) separately [CO-Section 4.1.2].
L327: ## Criteria for response and relapse:
L328: **Major Molecular response: MMR:** BCR::ABL1 (IS) \le 0.1%,
L329: **Deep Molecular Response: MR4.0:** BCR::ABL1 (IS) \le 0.01% or, **MR4.5:** BCR::ABL1 (IS) \le 0.0032%.
L330: **Cytogenetic response: CCyR:** No Ph-positive metaphases
L331: **CHR:** Defined as all of the following present for \ge 4 weeks: complete normalization of peripheral blood counts with leukocyte count &lt;10 \times 10^9/L, platelet count &lt;450 \times 10^9/L, basophils
L332: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L333: &lt;5%, no blasts and promyelocytes in peripheral blood, myelocytes + metamyelocytes &lt;5% in peripheral blood, and no evidence of extramedullary disease, including spleen and liver [CO-Table 4-2].
L334: ## **Efficacy analyses terminology** [CO-Section 4.1.2]:
L335: - ASCIMA – refers to participants randomized to the asciminib arm, within the stratum of participants with imatinib as their PRS-TKI
- ASC2G – refers to participants randomized to the asciminib arm, within the stratum of participants with 2G TKI as their PRS-TKI
- IS-TKIIMA – refers to participants randomized to the IS-TKI arm, within the stratum of participants with imatinib as their PRS-TKI
- IS-TKI2G – refers to participants randomized to the IS-TKI arm, within the stratum of participants with 2G TKI as their PRS-TKI
L336: **Datasets:** The datasets used to derive the tables and figures for Study J12301 are provided in Module 5.3.5.1 and datasets used for the summary documents including Exposure-Response and PopPK report are provided in Module 5.3.5.3 of this application.
L337: ## **Handling of missing assessments for primary analysis**
L338: Participants with missing RQ-PCR evaluation at the Week 48 visit were imputed as MMR responders if they had non-missing RQ-PCR evaluations at both Week 36 and Week 60 visits, and both met the MMR criteria (BCR::ABL1 levels (IS) ≤ 0.1%), assuming that MMR was maintained between Week 36 and Week 60. If RQ-PCR evaluations were performed at unscheduled visits closer to the Week 48 visit (before or after), these were taken into account for the imputation. Otherwise, participants with missing RQ-PCR evaluations at Week 48 were considered as non-responders [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 9.7.3.4]
L339: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L340: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L373: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L374: The FAS consisted of all 405 randomized participants (Table 6). Four participants (1 in the asciminib arm and 3 in the IS-TKI arm) never received any study treatment. Of 201 participants receiving IS-TKIs, 99 received imatinib, 49 received nilotinib, 42 received dasatinib, and 11 received bosutinib, respectively [CO-Section 4.1.3.2].
L375: Asciminib vs. IS-TKI (FAS): As of the data cut-off date, the proportion of participants who discontinued study treatment was lower in the asciminib arm (13.4%) compared to the IS-TKI arm (29.9%). Overall, treatment discontinuations were predominantly due to lack of efficacy or AEs; both were less frequent in the asciminib arm than in the IS-TKI arm (unsatisfactory therapeutic effect: 6.0% vs. 15.2%; AE: 5.5% vs. 10.3%). Among treatment discontinuations due to unsatisfactory therapeutic effect the majority were due to treatment failure per ELN criteria 2020 (5.0% in the asciminib arm vs. 11.8% in the IS-TKI arm) (Table 6).
L376: A similar trend was observed for study treatment discontinuations (overall and for individual reasons) in the FASIMA and FAS2GTKI, with the proportions of participants with study treatment discontinuations being lower in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (Table 6).
L377: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L378: FDA confirms the Applicant's position. One patient in the asciminib arm was not treated because of subject decision, and three patients in the IS-TKI arm were not treated because of subject decision, adverse event, and screen failure (one for each).
L379: Treatment discontinuation due to various reasons was less frequent with asciminib vs. IS-TKIs (4.5% vs. 7.4%). The most frequent reasons for treatment discontinuation in both arms were unsatisfactory therapeutic effect (6% vs. 15.2%) and adverse event (5.5% vs. 10.3%).

L386: ## The Applicant's Position:
L387: Overall, the proportion of participants with protocol deviations (PDs) was similar in the asciminib arm (47.3%) compared to the IS-TKI arm (48.0%) (Table 7). There were 3 participants (1 participant in the asciminib arm and 2 participants in the IS-TKI arm) who discontinued study treatment due to PD. None of the PDs resulted in exclusion of data from the analyses and there was no impact on the primary and secondary endpoints due to these PDs [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 10.2].
L388: Overall, the PDs specific to the COVID-19 pandemic were comparable between the asciminib and IS-TKI arms (8.5% vs. 11.8%). The majority of these deviations were assessment/procedure not done, visit done outside of study site, missed visit, and assessment/procedure changed due to COVID-19. The frequency and nature of the COVID-19 related PDs in imatinib and 2G TKI strata were similar to that reported overall [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 10.2.1].
L389: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L390: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Protocol deviations were similar in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (47.3% vs. 48%). Protocol deviation category of "Other" was less frequent with asciminib (31.8% vs. 35.3%). Protocol deviation categories of "Selection criteria not met" and "Treatment deviation" were more frequent with asciminib compared to
L391: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L392: IS-TKIs, respectively, 14.9% vs. 12.3% and 10.9% vs. 7.8%. The protocol deviations were considered to be unlikely to bias the study in favor of the study drug.

L402: ## The Applicant's Position:
L403: The demographic characteristics of the study population were well balanced between treatment arms, both overall and within each stratum. The study population was representative of the intended target population. Of note, compared to participants in the 2G TKI stratum, participants in the imatinib stratum were older in age, had a lower proportion of participants in the ELTS high-risk group, had a higher proportion of participants in the high-risk category for Framingham score, and had a higher proportion of participants with CCI \ge 5. This is reflective of medical practice where there is preferential use of imatinib in patients with similar demographic profile: older patients, in patients with cardiovascular comorbidities and patients in the low ELTS risk group (Table 8) (NCCN 2024).
L404: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L405: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. There were more male patients than female patients in both the asciminib and IS-TKI arms. FDA notes that Black or African American patients are significantly underrepresented compared to patients with CML-CP in the US. There was also underrepresentation of Hispanic or Latino patients in both arms. In the US, 81% of CML patients identify as White, 12% as Black or African American, 4% as Other, and 3% as Asian (Sobieski et al. 2022). In addition, the majority of study subjects were enrolled at ex-US sites. As Black or African American and Hispanic/Latino patients with CML are underrepresented in study CABL001J12301, FDA plans to issue a PMC to determine whether asciminib shows a similar safety and efficacy profile in these racial and ethnic groups.
L406: Demographics were well balanced between arms.

L413: ## The Applicant's Position:
L414: The disease history and other baseline characteristics of the study population were well balanced between treatment arms, both overall and within each stratum (Table 9).
L415: Characteristics of bone marrow (BM) aspirate analysis at baseline were well balanced between the two treatment arms, both overall and within the imatinib stratum and 2G TKI stratum. The mean blasts and mean blasts + promyelocytes in bone marrow were both similar for participants between the two treatment arms. More than 90% of participants did not have additional chromosomal abnormalities in Ph+ metaphases or Ph- metaphases for either of the arms [CO-Section 4.1.3.1].
L416: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L417: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. More patients in the asciminib arm used prior TKIs compared to the IS-TKI arm (11.9% vs. 4.9%). However, the length of exposure (1.8 weeks in the asciminib arm and 1.9 weeks in the IS-TKI arm) was too short to influence the treatment outcomes at week 48. Other baseline characteristics were well balanced between the arms.
L418: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L419: ## The Applicant's Position:
L420: Treatment compliance was assured by the Investigator and/or study personnel. The majority of participants received study treatment at the planned dose. The median RDI was 100.0% (range: 49.9% - 100%) for the asciminib arm and was 99.7% (range: 23.8% 100.2%) for the IS-TKI arm [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 10.6.2].
L421: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L422: Overall, 94.0% of participants in the asciminib arm compared to 95.5% of participants in the IS-TKI arm required concomitant medications and the nature of concomitant medications was similar in participants across the two treatment arms [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 10.5.2].
L423: Three participants (1.5%) in the asciminib group and 4 participants (2.0%) in the IS-TKI group overall received hydroxycarbamide and one participant (1.0%) in the 2GTKI group received anagrelide [Study J12301 PA CSR-Section 10.5.3].
L424: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L425: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Treatment compliance, use of concomitant medications, and use of rescue medications was similar between the arms. Use of concomitant medications (hydroxyurea, anagrelide, imatinib) with asciminib was not associated with any specific TEAEs.
