L440: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L441: ## The Applicant's Position:
L442: The study met both its primary objectives (as described in Table 10) and was declared positive. Clinically relevant and highly statistically significant improvements in the primary endpoint of MMR rate at Week 48 were observed in participants treated with asciminib vs. participants treated with IS-TKI overall (imatinib, nilotinib, dasatinib, or bosutinib), and vs. participants with imatinib as their pre-randomization selected-TKI (PRS-TKI).
L443: MMR at Week 48 -Asciminib vs. IS-TKI: Superiority was demonstrated for asciminib 80 mg QD compared to IS-TKI, in the overall participants and within the stratum of participants who had imatinib as their PRS-TKI, for the primary endpoint of MMR at Week 48 while on study treatment without meeting any treatment failure criteria (as per ELN 2020 criteria) and without treatment discontinuation. The MMR rate at Week 48 was 67.7% in the asciminib arm compared to 49.0% in the IS-TKI arm. The common treatment difference in the MMR rate was 18.88%, which was clinically meaningful and highly statistically significant (95% CI: 9.59, 28.17; one-sided adjusted p-value: &lt;0.001, per the CMH one-sided test, stratified by both ELTS risk group and PRS-TKI (imatinib vs. 2G TKI) (Table 10).
L444: Results of the predefined sensitivity analysis of MMR rate at Week 48 (without imputation) of the primary objectives were consistent with the primary results. The MMR rate at Week 48 (without imputation) was 67.2% in the asciminib arm and 48.0% in the IS-TKI arm with the common treatment difference in the MMR rate of 19.37% (95% CI: 10.09, 28.64) [SCE-Section 3.2.1.1].
L445: Two supplemental analyses of MMR rate at Week 48 (FAS) 1) stratified with PRS-TKI and ELTS category from eCRF [asciminib arm vs. IS-TKI arm: 67.7% vs. 49.0%, common treatment difference: 18.51% (95% CI: 9.26, 27.75) 2) after excluding 3 participants from the IS-TKI arm (Imatinib stratum): one randomized by mistake, one had an atypical transcript at screening and was in MMR at baseline, and one participant had typical and atypical transcript at screening [asciminib arm vs. IS-TKI arm: 67.7% vs. 49.3%, common treatment difference: 18.73% (95% CI: 9.39, 28.07)] were consistent with those from the primary analysis indicating no significant impact on the interpretation of the primary efficacy results [SCE-Section 3.2.1.1].
L446: Multivariate analyses indicated a consistent treatment effect of asciminib compared to IS-TKI, independent of the demographic and prognostic variables tested [CO-Section 4.1.4.1.1]. Subgroup analyses for rate of MMR at Week 48 (FAS) demonstrated a robust and consistent treatment effect in favour of asciminib across all demographic and prognostic subgroups, including ELTS risk group (low, intermediate, high) (Figure 4).
L447: MMR at Week 48-ASCIMA vs. IS-TKIIMA: Within the imatinib stratum, superiority was demonstrated for asciminib 80 mg QD compared to IS-TKI for the primary endpoint of MMR at Week 48 (Table 10). Results of sensitivity analysis of MMR rate at Week 48 without imputation, were consistent with the primary results confirming robustness of the results. The results of the multivariate
L448: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L449: analysis indicate a consistent treatment effect favoring ASCIMA vs. IS-TKIIMA, independent of the demographic and prognostic variables tested [CO-Section 4.1.4.1.2].
L450: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L451: FDA confirms that the primary results and associated subgroup results are correct. FDA notes that the overall treatment effect on MMR at Week 48 is a difference of 18.88%, while the difference in the imatinib stratum is 29.55%. As noted below in the Applicant's efficacy results of secondary endpoints, this difference was 8.17% for the 2nd generation TKI stratum. This was expected a priori, as all of the 2nd generation TKIs included were approved based on an improvement in response rates as compared to imatinib in randomized trials.
L452: Given this heterogeneity of treatment effect, the overall estimate should not be considered to be representative of the comparative effectiveness of asciminib versus any of the comparators. The associated subgroup estimates should be considered more reliable for understanding the benefit of asciminib over imatinib or 2nd generation TKIs.
L453: The MMR rate at 48 weeks for imatinib is 40.20%. A recent trial in patients with newly-diagnosed CP-CML to support an approval was BFORE, which randomized patients 1:1 to bosutinib or imatinib. The MMR rate at 48 weeks for imatinib was 37% in BFORE, as reported in the bosutinib USPI. Response rates to imatinib in earlier trials are lower (22% for MMR at 12 months as reported in the nilotinib USPI and 33.8% for MMR at any time as reported in the dasatinib USPI). Such comparisons are limited by potential differences in populations and other uncontrolled confounders.
L454: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L455: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L456: No data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections.
L457: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L458: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment as no data integrity concerns were found. The data were submitted on April 30, 2024 (Data as of 11/28/2023) under \\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA215358\0109. The review team was able to replicate the primary and secondary efficacy results, safety results, and key demographic results. No inconsistencies were identified.

L482: ## The Applicant's Position:
L483: MMR at Week 48-ASC2G vs. IS-TKI2G: Within the 2G TKI stratum, the MMR rate at Week 48 in the asciminib arm was numerically higher at 66.0% compared to 57.8% in the IS-TKI arm. The common treatment difference in the MMR rate was 8.17% (95% CI: -5.14, 21.47) (not powered). A numerically higher treatment effect in favor of asciminib was observed across all demographic and prognostic subgroups, including all ELTS risk categories. Results of the predefined sensitivity analyses (without imputation) were consistent with the analysis of MMR rate at Week 48 in FAS2GTKI with imputation, thereby confirming robustness of the results [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.1].
L484: MMR at and by all scheduled data collection time points: MMR at and by scheduled data collection time points: A higher rate of MMR was achieved in participants in the asciminib arm (overall and within the imatinib and 2G TKI strata) compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm at or by each scheduled timepoint assessed, with clinically meaningful differences starting to be observed from Week 12 onwards. The results are consistent across the imatinib and 2G TKI strata, favouring asciminib [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.1].
L485: The MMR rate at and by each scheduled time point (Weeks 12, 24, 36, and 48) was higher for the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm, with clinically meaningful differences starting as early as Week 12 (MMR rate at Week 12: 29.9% in the asciminib arm compared to 7.4% in the IS-
L486: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L487: TKI arm, with an estimated common treatment difference of 22.55% (95% CI: 15.32, 29.79)). This clinically meaningful difference in MMR rate favoring participants in the asciminib arm persisted through Week 48 (Table 11) [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.1].
L488: MR2.0 (BCR::ABL1 \le1%) at and by all scheduled data collection time points: A higher rate of MR2.0 was achieved in participants in the asciminib arm (overall and within the imatinib and 2G TKI stratum) compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm **at or by** each scheduled timepoint assessed, with clinically meaningful differences starting to be observed from as early as Week 8 onwards. The results are consistent across the imatinib and 2G TKI strata, favouring asciminib [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.1].
L489: The MR2.0 rate at and by each scheduled time point (Weeks 12, 24, 36 and 48) was higher for the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm, with clinically meaningful differences starting as early as Week 8 (MR2.0 rate at Week 8: 15.4% in the asciminib arm compared to 2.9% in the IS-TKI arm, with an estimated common treatment difference of 12.52% (95% CI: 7.02, 18.03)) which persisted through Week 48 (Table 11) [SCE-Table 3-13] [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.1].
L490: Time to MMR: The probability of achieving MMR earlier, as early as Week 12, was higher in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm overall (probability of achieving MMR by Week 48: 66.5% vs. 46.3%), and within the imatinib stratum (by Week 48: 69.1% vs. 38.0%) and the 2G TKI stratum (by Week 48: 64.0% vs. 54.5%) (Figure 5).
L491: MMR was achieved faster in participants randomized to the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm overall (KM estimated median time to first MMR: 24.3 weeks vs. 36.4 weeks) and the trends were similar across the imatinib (24.1 weeks vs. 48.6 weeks) and the 2G TKI strata (24.3 weeks vs. 36.1 weeks) (Table 11).
L492: EMR (BCR::ABL1 (IS) \le10% at Week 12): A higher number of participants in the asciminib arm achieved EMR compared to the IS-TKI arm (overall and within each stratum). EMR indicates an optimal response to TKI therapy and has been shown to correlate with good prognosis (improved OS and PFS) (Table 11).
L493: Deep molecular response (MR4.0/MR4.5): A higher rate of MR4.0 and MR4.5 was achieved in participants in the asciminib arm (overall and within the imatinib and 2G TKI stratum) compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm **at and by** each scheduled timepoint assessed, with clinically meaningful differences starting to be observed from Week 24. The results are consistent across the imatinib and 2G TKI strata, favouring asciminib (Table 11).
L494: Time to MR4.0/MR4.5: MR4.0/MR4.5 was achieved faster in participants treated with asciminib compared to IS-TKI, and the results were similar within the imatinib and 2G TKI strata. Median time to first MR4.0 was 60.1 weeks in the asciminib arm and not reached in the IS-TKI arm [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.1].
L495: BCR::ABL1 ratio (% IS) kinetics: Summary plots over time of number of participants achieving EMR, MR2.0, MMR, MR4.0 and MR4.5 in FAS, FASIMA, and FAS2GTKI showed that higher proportion
L496: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L497: of participants in the asciminib arm achieved these thresholds earlier and as compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm. Overall and within each stratum, on average, participants in the asciminib arm had lower BCR::ABL1 levels as compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm, and from Week 12 onwards, the graphical comparison of medians at each timepoint for the asciminib vs. IS-TKI arm showed clinically meaningful difference (for FAS and FAS_{IMA}) and a numerically higher difference (for FAS_{2GTKI}) in favor of asciminib [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.2] (Figure 6).
L498: **CHR:** By Week 48, CHR was achieved in 94.5% of participants in the asciminib arm and 94.1% in the IS-TKI arm, with no clinical difference. A trend of earlier CHR being achieved in the asciminib arm before Week 8 was noted. Similar results were observed within the imatinib stratum (ASC^{IMA} vs. IS-TKI^{IMA}: 90.10% vs. 93.14%) and within the 2G TKI stratum (ASC^{2G} vs. IS-TKI^{2G}: 99.0% vs. 95.1%) [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.2].
L499: **CCyR by Week 48:** During the course of the study, cytogenetic assessment was not mandatory and only performed if clinically indicated (e.g., treatment failure). As a result, very few participants (n=2) had the cytogenetic assessment performed by Week 48 [Study J12301-PA-CSR-Section 11.3.3.1].
L500: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L501: FDA agrees with the results presented above. FDA focused on the validation of key primary results for MMR at Week 48.
L502: FDA notes that the MMR rate at 12 months for the 2GTKI stratum is 57.84% in the control arm. This is higher than the response rates reported in the corresponding USPI's for these products. For instance, bosutinib yielded a 47% MMR rate at 12 months and nilotinib yielded a 44% MMR rate at 12 months. The USPI for dasatinib reports an MMR rate at any time of 52.1%.

L517: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L518: ## The Applicant's Position:
L519: Treatment with asciminib should continue, until unacceptable toxicity or treatment failure occurs.
L520: Time to treatment failure (TTF): The KM probability of experiencing treatment failure by data cut-off was lower in the asciminib arm as compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm (FAS) (overall and within strata) [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.3].
L521: Event free survival (EFS) and failure free survival (FFS): The proportion of participants with an EFS event was approximately 2-times lower in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (12.4% vs. 24.5%). The probability of EFS and FFS was higher in participants in the asciminib arm as compared to participants in the IS-TKI arm (overall and within each stratum) [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.3].
L522: Progression free survival (PFS)/ Overall survival (OS) were immature at the time of this report. PFS events occurred in 2.5% of participants in the asciminib arm and 3.9% of participants in the IS-TKI arm. There were no on-treatment deaths in either treatment arm; 2/201 (1.0%) participants in the asciminib arm and 3/204 (1.5%) participants in the IS-TKI arm died during the study [CO-Section 4.1.4.2.3].
L523: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L524: FDA agrees with the results presented above. FDA focused on the validation of key primary results for MMR at Week 48. Therefore, all other results have not been completely validated.

L536: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L537: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L538: Efficacy results for the newly diagnosed Philadelphia chromosome positive CML in chronic phase indication was provided by study CABL001J12301. Study CABL001J12301 is a phase 3, multi-center, open-label, randomized study of asciminib versus IS-TKIs in participants with newly diagnosed Ph+ CP-CML. The primary endpoint of MMR at 48 weeks was considered appropriate as it reflects an endpoint likely to predict longer term disease control and survival and represents an acceptable depth and duration of response in the newly diagnosed setting. FDA agrees that the open-label design is appropriate given the differences in treated regimens and given the primary endpoint was assessed by central lab determined BCR-ABL1 levels and therefore not likely to be subject to measurement bias. The review team recommends that this indication receives accelerated approval.
L539: The Applicant also requested the Philadelphia chromosome-positive CML in CP, previously treated with two or more TKIs indication to be updated to previously treated Philadelphia chromosome-positive CML in CP indication to be inclusive of second line treatment. Efficacy results for the initial approval in 3L+ treatment was provided by study CABL001A2301 which was a phase 3, multi-center, open-label, randomized study of asciminib vs. bosutinib in patients with Philadelphia chromosome-positive CML in CP, previously treated with two or more TKIs. Study CABL001AUS08 is an ongoing phase 2, US multi-center, open label, single arm dose escalation study in patients with Philadelphia chromosome positive CML in CP receiving asciminib monotherapy as 2L or 1L treatment. The primary endpoint of this study is MMR rate at 12 months with secondary endpoints of molecular responses at other time points. For the
L540: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L541: previously treated Philadelphia chromosome-positive CML in CP indication, 6-month interim efficacy results in 28 patients using asciminib as 2L treatment were evaluated and found acceptable. See summary results at the interim analysis in Section 8.1.4. The review team recommends that the previously treated Philadelphia chromosome positive CML in CP indication receives regular approval.
L542: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L543: ## The Applicant's Position:
L544: Efficacy claims for the proposed indication are based on the results of the primary analysis of the Phase III registration Study J12301.
L545: Preliminary clinical data from Study CABL001AUS08 (ASC2ESCALATE, hereafter referred to as Study AUS08), an ongoing Phase II, US multi-center, open label, single arm dose escalation study of asciminib 80 mg QD single agent in 2L Ph+ CML-CP, are available and have been included as agreed with US FDA [CO-Section 1.0].
L546: The demographic, disease history and other baseline characteristics of the study population in the 2L cohort in Study AUS08 are representative of participants with Ph+ CML-CP in the 2L setting.
L547: As of the cut-off date for this analysis (22-Mar-2024), preliminary efficacy data from Study AUS08 (2L cohort; N=43) was analyzed for 14 participants who were evaluable for the 6 months efficacy assessment with the remaining 29 participants ongoing on study not having achieved this milestone yet. The analysis showed achievement of MR2.0 and MMR for asciminib in 12/14 (85.7%) (95% CI: 57.2, 98.2) and 8/14 (57.1%) (95% CI: 28.9, 82.3) participants respectively at 6 months. The MR4.0 rate was achieved in 4/14 participants (28.6%; 95% CI: 8.4, 58.1) at 6 months [CO-Section 4.2.2].
L548: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L549: FDA confirms the Applicant's position. Also, refer to Sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.3.
L550: During the review, updated safety and efficacy information was provided on August 30, 2024, for Study AUS08 from interim analysis 2 (IA2) with a data cutoff of June 28, 2024. At IA2, 71 patients were enrolled in the 2L cohort, and 28 patients were evaluable for efficacy at 6 months having completed the efficacy assessment at that timepoint or discontinued prior to the timepoint.
L551: Of the 28 patients evaluable at 6 months, 25 patients completed the 6-month visit, 1 patient had an unscheduled visit at day 145 which was considered to be the 6-month assessment, and 2 patients discontinued prior to the 6-month visit (1 due to an adverse event, 1 due to patient decision). The median age was 48 years with 25% of patients \ge65 years, 54% were male, and 93% were White. The reason for discontinuing the prior TKI was lack of efficacy in 16 (57%) patients and lack of tolerability in 12 (43%) patients.
L552: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L553: The MMR rate at 6 months was 43% (12/28; 95% CI: 24, 63). For patients who discontinued their prior TKI due to lack of efficacy, the MMR rate at 6 months was 31% (5/16; 95% CI: 11, 59). For patients who discontinued their prior TKI due to lack of tolerability, the MMR rate at 6 months was 58% (7/12; 95% CI: 28, 85).
L554: In the patients treated with asciminib as 2L treatment in this study, the MMR rate at 6 months was intermediate to the MMR rate observed in the 1L cohort in study J12301 and the 3L+ cohort in study A2301, which would be generally expected for this population. See Section 6.2.2.1 for discussion of PK/PD modeling used to support extrapolation to the 2L indication.
L555: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L556: ## The Applicant's Position:
L557: The efficacy evaluation for this indication is based on the primary analysis from Study J12301.
L558: This study met both primary objectives. The MMR rate at Week 48 in the asciminib arm vs. the IS-TKI arm in the overall population (FAS) was 67.7% vs. 49.0% and within the imatinib stratum (FASIMA) was 69.3% vs. 40.2%. The common treatment difference in the MMR rates (per the CMH one-sided test) between asciminib and IS-TKI were 18.88% (95% CI: 9.59, 28.17; p &lt; 0.001) (FAS) and 29.55% (95% CI: 16.91, 42.18; p &lt; 0.001) (FASIMA), which were both considered clinically meaningful and highly statistically significant.
L559: For both the overall population (FAS) and for the imatinib stratum (FASIMA), the subgroup analysis for rate of MMR at Week 48 demonstrated a robust and consistent treatment effect in favor of asciminib across all demographic and prognostic subgroups, including ELTS risk group (low, intermediate, high). Results of multivariate analyses also indicated a consistent treatment effect in favor of asciminib compared to IS-TKI, independent of the demographic and prognostic variables tested.
L560: The efficacy of asciminib was further demonstrated by other secondary efficacy endpoints of the study with data across all endpoints consistently favoring asciminib and showing an overall positive efficacy data for asciminib vs. the standard of care treatments.
L561: Higher and favorable rates with faster, sustained, and deeper molecular responses were observed generally in participants with asciminib treatment compared to the IS-TKIs, for molecular response (at and by all time-points).
L562: Within the 2G TKI stratum, the MMR rate at Week 48 in the asciminib arm was numerically higher than in the IS-TKI arm (66.0% vs. 57.8%) with the common treatment difference in the MMR rate being 8.17% (95% CI: -5.14%, 21.47%) (not powered) per the CMH one-sided test. A numerically higher treatment effect in favor of asciminib was observed across all demographic and prognostic subgroups, including all ELTS risk categories.
L563: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L564: Of note, the MMR rate at 12 months for asciminib was observed to be even better than what was predicted as part of the protocol design. This trend in improved MMR rates were also seen for the 2G TKIs including comparing with what was previously seen in literature, which could be attributed to the current much improved understanding of CML disease management.
L565: MMR was achieved faster overall and within each stratum, as early as Week 12. MMR was achieved faster in participants in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (median time to first MMR: 24.3 weeks vs. 36.4 weeks). The same trend was observed within the imatinib stratum (24.1 weeks vs. 48.6 weeks) and within the 2G TKI stratum (24.3 weeks vs. 36.1 weeks).
L566: Loss of MMR was rare in both treatment arms. All participants who achieved MMR continued to remain in MMR at the time of their last molecular assessment except 1 participant who had confirmed loss of MMR in the asciminib arm, suggesting a sustained treatment effect of asciminib.
L567: A higher rate of EMR (BCR::ABL1 \le 10\% at Week 12), and a higher rate of MR2.0 (BCR::ABL1 \le 1\%) **at** and **by** all scheduled timepoints from as early as Week 8 which persisted through Week 48, were also achieved in participants in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm, overall and within each stratum.
L568: Deep molecular response was achieved with higher rates of MR4.0 and MR4.5 (overall and within each stratum) observed in participants in the asciminib arm **at** and **by** all scheduled timepoints from as early as Week 24 which persisted through Week 48. Probability of achieving MR4.0/MR4.5 earlier, as early as Week 24, was higher in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (overall and within each stratum). Median time to first MR4.0 was 60.1 weeks in the asciminib arm and not reached in the IS-TKI arm. The majority of participants in the asciminib and the IS-TKI arm who achieved MR4.0 (96.9% vs. 98.2%)/MR4.5 (98.2% vs. 96.8%) continued to remain in MR4.0/MR4.5 at the time of their last molecular assessment, further suggesting a sustained treatment effect of asciminib.
L569: ## Overall Summary
L570: **Approval in the 3L+ setting:** Asciminib is approved for treatment of patients previously treated with at least two prior TKIs based on Study A2301 (Rea et al 2021, Hochhaus et al 2023).
L571: **Benefit/risk profile in the 1L setting:** Data from Study J12301 now shows compelling evidence supporting the efficacy claim of asciminib in adult patients with Ph+ CML-CP in the 1L setting.
L572: **Clinical extrapolation of efficacy of asciminib in 2L:** Based on an established response rate in 1L (from current Study J12301) and in 3L+ (from the previous Study A2301), efficacy of asciminib in 2L can be established by extrapolation. Taking into account historical data generated for the other TKIs, leading to approvals in the 2L setting and subsequently in the 1L setting, the indication of asciminib is proposed to be extended to previously treated patients including the 2L setting. The 2L Ph+ CML-CP treatment landscape includes the same TKIs as 1L, and the response rate for asciminib in 3L+ is comparable to that seen in 2L with 2G TKIs:
L573: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L574: - The 2L Ph+ CML-CP treatment landscape is composed of imatinib and 2G TKIs (dasatinib, nilotinib and bosutinib), as it is the case in the 1L setting. Since Study J12301 demonstrates the superiority of asciminib against the IS-TKIs of imatinib and 2G TKIs in 1L Ph+ CML-CP, a similar trend of response could be expected in 2L.
- Furthermore, the response rate observed with asciminib in the 3L+ population (MMR by 2 years: 41%; Hochhaus et al 2023) is even better than the response rate seen in pivotal studies with 2G TKIs in 2L (MMR by 2 years: 28 to 38%; nilotinib, Kantarjian et al 2011; dasatinib, Shah et al 2010; bosutinib, Gambacorti-Passerini et al 2014).
L575: Considering the above and also the fact that higher efficacy of TKIs is seen in earlier lines compared to later lines, it can be concluded that asciminib would be effective and compare favorably to other TKIs in 2L Ph+ CML-CP.
L576: **Modeling based estimated efficacy in 2L extrapolation data:** Furthermore, model-based analysis of asciminib based on 1L and 3L+ data as well as the nilotinib 1L and 2L modeling data used for extrapolation across lines of asciminib treatment in CML-CP was able to successfully show high predicted MMR rates for asciminib in 2L and as expected, in-between the MMR rates demonstrated in 1L and 3L+ clinical studies: a 48-week MMR rate in the range of 54-63% and a 96-week MMR rate of 66-74% was estimated for asciminib in 2L. Thus, asciminib is expected to be highly efficacious as a 2L treatment for Ph+ CML-CP.
L577: **Interim preliminary results from Study AUS08:** The demographic, disease history and other baseline characteristics of the study population are representative of participants with Ph+ CML-CP in the 2L setting. Available clinical data from Study AUS08 shows preliminary robust efficacy of asciminib in 2L Ph+ CML-CP with MR2.0 and MMR for asciminib in 12/14 (85.7%) (95% CI: 57.2, 98.2) and 8/14 (57.1%) (95% CI: 28.9, 82.3) participants respectively at 6 months. This efficacy data in 2L is trending to validate the clinical extrapolation that efficacy rates in 2L can be estimated to be between the observed rates in 1L and 3L+ and is also in alignment with the data predicted by the 2L modeling approach. Within the limitation of the small dataset this preliminary data seems to compare favorably with historical data from 2G TKIs in 2L, where 25% to 50% rate of MCyR by 6 months, and ~ 7-11% MMR rate at 6 months have been reported (Gambacorti-Passerini et al 2014).
L578: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L579: Refer to the FDA's Assessment in Section 8.1.3.
L580: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L581: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L582: ## The Applicant's Position:
L583: The key safety data in support of this application is from the registration Study J12301 (N=401 treated participants; 200 in the asciminib treatment group and 201 in the IS-TKI treatment group).
L584: The safety evaluation of asciminib monotherapy to establish the overall safety profile of asciminib, is based on data from 556 participants with Ph+ CML in a pooled analysis across 3 clinical studies: Study J12301 (1L), n=200; Study A2301 (3L+), n=156, and Study X2101, n=200. This Safety pool was pre-agreed with FDA. The pooling strategy supports the proposed indication covering the overall disease population, i.e. adult participants with Ph+ CML-CP, irrespective of line of therapy. Participants harboring the T315I mutation were also included.
L585: All safety analyses were performed on the Safety Set. The Safety Set includes all participants who received at least one dose of study treatment in Studies J12301, A2301, and X2101. Participants were analyzed according to the study treatment received. In Studies J12301 and A2301, treatment received is defined as the randomized treatment if the participant took at least one dose of asciminib or comparator TKI. Participants who switched treatment were not included.
L586: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L587: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position of the safety database for asciminib. Warnings and Precautions sections of the proposed USPI is based on the Asciminib Safety Pool (N=556). The Asciminib Safety Pool consists of patients treated with asciminib in three studies: 200 patients in study CABL001J12301 (1L), 156 patients in study CABL001A2301 (3L+), and 200 patients in study CABL001X2101.
L588: The analyses in this review are based on study CABL001J12301 with a data cut-off date of 28 November 2023. Safety analyses of the interim results from the 2L cohort of 71 patients in study CABL001AUS08 with a data cut-off date of 28 June 2024 have also been performed and included in relevant sections of the review.
L589: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L590: ## The Applicant's Position:
L591: A comprehensive assessment of safety data relevant to the use of asciminib as monotherapy in participants with CML is provided in the subsequent sections below (Section 8.2.4).
L592: Individual safety data from Study J12301 (N=401 treated participants; 200 in the asciminib arm and 201 in the IS-TKI arm) is also summarized in comparison to IS-TKIs (all comparators:
L593: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L594: imatinib, nilotinib, dasatinib or bosutinib), imatinib and 2G TKIs and is essential for a thorough benefit-risk assessment of asciminib vs. the current therapies (imatinib and the 2G TKIs).
L595: Additionally, long-term safety from Studies A2301 and X2101 are included in the Safety pool.
L596: The data from these studies allow for a comprehensive and robust assessment of the safety profile of asciminib monotherapy and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in participants with Ph+ CML-CP. This safety population is also considered appropriate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management in the intended population.
L597: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L598: As the Asciminib Safety Pool (N=556) includes safety data related to a larger patient population of patients using asciminib, Warnings &amp; Precautions section of the label will describe adverse events of special interest in this patient population. Otherwise, safety data in this review and the USPI will be mostly based on the patient populations in study CABL001J12301 (safety set of 200 patients in the asciminib arm and 201 in the IS-TKI arm). Interim safety data from 71 patients treated with asciminib as 2L treatment in study CABL001AUS08 have also been analyzed to support the previously treated Ph+ CP-CML indication.

L605: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L606: ## The Applicant's Position:
L607: A pooled analysis of 556 participants contributed to the main analysis for safety of asciminib. Exposure to asciminib in Studies J12301, A2301 and X2101 was considered appropriate by Novartis to allow for an adequate assessment of the safety of asciminib in participants who are representative of the intended target population.
L608: **In Study J12301**, the median duration of exposure to study drug was marginally longer in the asciminib treatment group (69.79 weeks) compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (64.29 weeks) (Table 133). Within the IS-TKI treatment group, the median duration of exposure was 56.29 weeks for imatinib and 71.43 weeks for 2G TKI. The difference in exposure between imatinib and 2G TKI is due in part to the recruitment dynamics wherein recruitment in the 2G TKI stratum was completed early. After enrollment of the 2G TKI stratum was completed, all participants were entered into the imatinib stratum during the last 3 months. As a result, the imatinib stratum has lower exposure as well as shorter follow-up [CO-Section 5.1.1.3].
L609: In the **All Asciminib Safety Pool**, the median duration of exposure (83.3 weeks, range: 0.1 to 439.0) and the total exposure (1407.1 patient-years) is considered adequate for a comprehensive safety assessment for asciminib across the different lines of treatment (Table 133).
L610: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L611: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the duration of exposure to asciminib is adequate considering that the dual primary endpoints are MMR at 48 weeks with asciminib versus IS-TKI and MMR at 48 weeks with asciminib versus imatinib (both within the stratum of patients with imatinib as their pre-randomization selected TKI).
L612: ## **Relevant characteristics of the safety population:**
L613: ## The Applicant's Position:
L614: Overall, the demographics and baseline characteristics in the All Asciminib Safety Pool were representative of the targeted patient population of newly-diagnosed and previously-treated Ph+ CML-CP.
L615: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L616: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Asciminib Safety Pool (N=556) included sufficient number of patients to reflect the demographic and baseline characteristics of newly diagnosed and previously treated Ph+ CP-CML patients.
L617: ## **Adequacy of the safety database:**
L618: The Applicant's Position: Details are provided in Section 8.2.
L619: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L620: The size of the safety database is adequate to provide a reasonable estimate of the adverse
L621: reactions, and the duration of treatment is adequate to allow assessment of adverse reactions over time for the indicated populations.
L622: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L623: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L624: ## The Applicant's Position:
L625: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported; data in the CRFs and adverse event databases were consistent.
L626: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L627: The quality of the safety data submitted was adequate to allow substantial primary review.
L628: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L629: ## The Applicant's Position:
L630: The safety of asciminib was evaluated based on:
L631: - Frequency, type, and severity of AEs (graded as per NCI CTCAE version 5.0 for Study J12301, and version 4.03 for Studies A2301 and X2101) and study drug relationship. (Note: grade 5 events were collected in Studies J12301 and A2301 and were derived for Study X2101 based on events with a fatal outcome, and incorporated in the Asciminib Safety Pool).
- Frequency of deaths, SAEs, AESIs, and ADRs.
- Changes in laboratory parameters, with particular attention to grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities, ECG changes, and vital signs.
L632: Adverse events were coded using MedDRA version 26.1 for Study J12301, and the same version was applied to all studies in the All Asciminib Safety Pool regardless of the individual study MedDRA versions.
L633: Participants were questioned about AEs at each clinical visit. In addition, AEs could also be detected when reported by the participants during or between visits or through physical examination, laboratory test results, or other assessments. Further to the standard safety evaluations outlined above, AE categories expected to be associated with asciminib and other TKIs were also analyzed. These Adverse events of special interest (AESIs) were identified by grouping AEs that are of scientific and medical concern specific to asciminib and/or are related to class risks of other TKIs.
L634: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L635: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L636: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Adverse events were assessed by frequency (i.e., events per patient). Additional grouped terms were evaluated based on common related terms (see Appendix 19.5). The grouped terms are included in safety analyses throughout the review and will be indicated with an "*" or "(GT)" in safety tables and the text of the review. The same grouped terms were used for safety analyses throughout the label.
L637: Adverse events in study CABL001AUS08 were graded as per CTCAE version 5.0 and they were coded using MedDRA version 27.0.
L638: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L639: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L640: Refer Section 8.1.1 for safety assessment in Study J12301. The nature and timing of the clinical monitoring of participant safety was considered adequate for the expected toxicities associated with the study treatments.
L641: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L642: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The frequency of clinical assessments is adequate to assess the risks of serious safety signals.
L643: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L644: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L645: **In Study J12301**, overall, asciminib showed a markedly favorable safety profile compared to IS-TKIs including imatinib and 2G TKIs (Table 20).
L646: In order to account for any differences in duration of exposure in Study J12301, analyses of exposure-adjusted incidence rate (EAIR) of AEs were also performed to compare the AE incidence between the treatment groups. The EAIR of AEs showed a similar distribution with regards to the most frequent adverse events, and the differences between treatment groups were also consistent with the results from the unadjusted AE incidence rates. Hence, the EAIR for events is not discussed in detail in this document [SCS-Section 2] [CO-Section 5.1.2.1.3].
L647: In the **All Asciminib Safety Pool**, the safety profile is consistent to that seen in Study J12301 (Table 20). No new or worsening safety concerns were identified in the All Asciminib Safety Pool and the safety findings were overall consistent with the known safety profile of asciminib. The differences, if any, between the asciminib treatment group in Study J12301 and the All Asciminib Safety Pool are expected due to the larger number of heavily pre-treated participants with resistant or intolerant disease, leading to heavier burden of baseline co-morbidities in Studies A2301 and X2101, unlike the participants in Study J12301.
L648: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L649: Detailed description of the All Asciminib Safety Pool will not be presented specifically for each of the sections in this document. However, pooled data in this document is presented in the outputs both in-text and additional details can be found in the [SCS-Section 2.0].

L660: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L661: ## The Applicant's Position:
L662: In Study J12301, as of the 28-Nov-2023 data cut-off date, there were no on-treatment deaths (i.e. death occurring during the treatment period and up to 30 days after the end of treatment) reported in any of the treatment groups. Overall, 5 (1.25%) participants subsequently died post the on-treatment period: 2 (1.0%) from the asciminib treatment group and 3 participants (1.5%) from the IS-TKI treatment group. The reasons for deaths are provided in Table 14, further details can be found in [SCS-Section 2.3].
L663: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L664: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of death due to adverse events. In Study CABL001J12301, no excess mortality within 30 days of initiation of treatment was observed with asciminib. FDA analysis showed that two deaths in the asciminib arm, and three deaths in the IS-TKI arm in study CABL001J12301 occurred 30 days after last dose of drug. The two deaths 30 days after the last dose of asciminib were caused by post procedural complication and metastatic bronchial carcinoma. The three deaths 30 days after the last dose of IS-TKI occurred with imatinib and were caused by disease indication, cor pulmonale, and metastatic gastric malignancy, respectively. FDA assesses that based on the death summaries provided the causality between asciminib and deaths seems to be not related (Table 15).
L665: **Table 15 Summary of death narratives &gt;30 days after last dose of asciminib in study CABL001J12301**
L666: USUBJID	Preferred term for death	Summary of death
(b) (6)	Post procedural complication (asciminib 80 mg qd)	The patient received allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplant 105 days after the last dose of asciminib. The last dose of asciminib was received on Day 218 and the patient died on Day 411 due to complications of hematopoietic stem cell transplant. The relationship is evaluated as being not related.
(b) (6)	Metastatic bronchial carcinoma (asciminib 80 mg qd)	The patient was a smoker (106 pack years). The patient received the last dose of asciminib on Day 168 and treatment was permanently discontinued due to hepatotoxicity on Day 195. The relationship is evaluated as being not related.

L683: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L684: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L685: In Study J12301, there were no cases of fatal outcome due to an SAE in any of the treatment groups (Table 20). Overall, the incidence of individual SAEs was low for both treatment groups. The incidence of SAEs was lower in the asciminib treatment group compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (11.0% vs. 15.9%, grade \ge 3 8.5% vs. 11.9%), including 2G TKI (19.6%, grade \ge 3 13.7%), and comparable to the imatinib (12.1%, grade \ge 3 10.1%) group (Table 16).
L686: Most of the SAEs were reported as single cases, except, in the: asciminib treatment group, large intestine perforation (2 participants, 1.0%); no grade \ge 3 SAEs occurred in more than 1 participant; IS-TKI treatment group: pneumonia (4 participants, 2.0%), COVID-19, pleural effusion and prostate cancer (2 participants, 1.0% each), and pneumonia was the only grade \ge 3 SAE that occurred in more than 1 participant (3 participants, 1.5%) [SCS-Section 2.4.1]. As most of the SAEs were reported as single cases, SAEs by system organ class is presented in Table 15 above. SAEs by preferred term are provided in [SCS Appendix 1-Table 2-9].
L687: The incidence of SAEs suspected to be study treatment related (per Investigator determination) was comparable between both asciminib and IS-TKI treatment groups (4.0% vs. 5.0%), including imatinib (4.0% vs. 4.0%) and 2G TKIs (4.0% vs. 5.9%). Except for pleural effusion (2 participants) in the 2G TKI group (none in the asciminib group), other suspected SAEs in both treatment groups were reported as single cases and no clinically meaningful comparisons could thus be derived [SCS-Section 2.4.3] [SCS Appendix 1-Table 2-8].
L688: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L689: FDA agrees with the Applicant about the frequency of SAEs according to System Organ Class (SOC) in the asciminib and IS-TKI arms of study CABL001J12301. FDA performed an analysis of all-grade treatment-emergent SAEs and events occurring in \ge 1\% in both arms of the study which are presented in Table 17. The Applicant used SOC terms, and the FDA groups preferred terms that describe the same concept. The overall rate of SAEs was lower in the asciminib arm than in the IS-TKI arm and there was not any SAE which occurred at a significantly higher frequency in the asciminib arm. SAEs in study CABL001J12301 are acceptable because they are infrequent, and the differences between the groups are not thought to affect the safety profile of asciminib.

L714: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L715: ## The Applicant's Position:
L716: In **Study J12301**, the incidence of AEs (all grades) leading to treatment discontinuation was lower with asciminib (4.5%) compared to IS-TKIs (10.4%; imatinib: 11.1%; 2G TKI: 9.8%) and the majority of the events (except for diarrhea in 1 participant) were assessed by the Investigator as being related to study treatment. Most of the AEs leading to discontinuation were reported as single cases in both the asciminib and IS-TKI treatment group except for lipase increased (1.5% vs. 0%) and thrombocytopenia (1.0% vs. 0%); diarrhea (0% vs. 2.0%) in the imatinib group; and pleural effusion (0% vs. 2.0%) in the 2G TKI group. The incidence of grade \ge 3 AEs leading to treatment discontinuation was lower with asciminib (3.0%) compared to IS-TKI (5.5%; imatinib: 6.1%; 2G TKI: 4.9%). All grade \ge 3 AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were reported as single cases (Table 19) [SCS-Section 2.5.1].
L717: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L718: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment about discontinuations in study CABL001J12301. The percentage of patients who discontinued treatment for all grade adverse events was lower in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (4.5% vs. 10.4%). FDA also considered GTs which led to discontinuation. Particular adverse events leading to discontinuation did not occur in more than 1.5% of patients in the asciminib arm. AEs which led to discontinuation in the asciminib arm were lipase increased (1.5%), thrombocytopenia (GT) (1%), cardiovascular toxicity (GT) (0.5%), neuropathy peripheral (GT) (0.5%), neutropenia (GT) (0.5%), pancreatitis (GT) (0.5%), and delirium (0.5%). We agree that none of the grade 3 or 4 discontinuations in the asciminib arm occurred in more than 0.5% of the patients.
L719: In study CABL001AUS08, one patient (1.4%) discontinued asciminib due to gastrointestinal toxicity (GT).
L720: ## **Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects**
L721: ## The Applicant's Position:
L722: In **Study J12301**, the proportion of participants with treatment-related AEs leading to dose reduction was markedly lower with asciminib compared to the IS-TKIs (5.5% vs. 18.4%) as well as imatinib (14.1%) and 2G TKI (22.5%). There were no events by PT reported with a \ge 5\% difference between asciminib and IS-TKI treatment groups (Table 20) [SCS-Section 2.5.3].
L723: The proportion of participants with treatment-related AEs requiring dose interruption was lower with asciminib compared to the IS-TKIs (21.5% vs. 35.8%) as well as imatinib (31.3%) and 2G TKI (40.2%). There were no treatment-related AEs leading to dose interruption reported more frequently in the asciminib treatment group with a \ge 5\% difference compared to the IS-TKI treatment group including imatinib. Compared to the 2G TKI group, ALT increased (1.0% vs. 7.8%) and anemia (0% vs. 4.9%) occurred with \ge 5\% difference (Table 20) [SCS-Section 2.5.2].
L724: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L725: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L726: In study CABL001J12301, the majority of dose modifications (dose interruptions and dose reductions) were dose interruptions. FDA analysis showed that dose interruption occurred in 30% of patients in asciminib arm and 39.8% of patients in the IS-TKI arm. The most frequent causes for dose interruption in &gt;5% of patients in the asciminib arm were thrombocytopenia (GT) (13%), and neutropenia (GT) (9.5%). FDA analysis also showed that adverse events leading to dose reduction occurred in 5.5% of patients in asciminib arm compared to 18.9% of patients in IS-TKI arm. The most frequent causes for dose reduction in the asciminib arm were thrombocytopenia (GT) (2.5%), and neutropenia (GT) (1.5%).
L727: In study CABL001AUS08, dose interruptions due to adverse events occurred in 18.3% of patients and dose reductions due to adverse events occurred in 2.8% of patients. Adverse events which led to dose modifications in &gt;1.4% of patients were thrombocytopenia (GT) (5.6%), fatigue (GT) (2.8%), and gastrointestinal toxicity (GT) (2.8%).
L728: ## Significant Adverse Events
L729: **The Applicant's Position:** The significant AEs reported are described above: SAEs, AEs leading to treatment discontinuation/dose interruption/reduction. Laboratory abnormalities are described below.
L730: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L731: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L747: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L748: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L749: The results from registration **Study J12301** show that the safety data are consistent with the known safety profile of asciminib. Overall, the AEs observed with asciminib treatment were as expected and could be attributed to the mechanism of action of asciminib and is in line with the current pre-clinical and clinical knowledge of asciminib. No unknown or unexpected safety concerns were observed. Adverse events reported with asciminib treatment appear to be manageable with appropriate monitoring and are reversible upon dose interruption and/or dose reduction of asciminib, and/or the use of supportive care [CO-Section 5.1.2].
L750: AEs occurred in 93.5% of participants treated with asciminib compared to 93.9% of participants treated with imatinib and 100% of participants treated with 2G TKIs (97.0% for IS-TKIs overall). Grade \ge 3 events were reported less frequently with asciminib (38.0%) compared to IS-TKIs (49.8%; imatinib: 44.4%; 2G TKI: 54.9%). AEs suspected to be study treatment related were reported less frequently in the asciminib treatment group (75.0%) compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (89.6%; imatinib: 83.8%; 2G TKI: 95.1%) (Table 218) [CO-Section 5.1.2.2.1].
L751: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L752: FDA analysis of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) aligns with that of the Applicant's except that FDA used GTs; however, the Applicant used PTs. Table 22 shows TEAEs \ge 10\%, all grades and grades 3-4, in study CABL001J12301. This table includes laboratory investigations which are generally underreported as adverse events and will be reported separately in labeling and are reviewed in the laboratory section below. Most TEAEs were less frequent in the asciminib arm. Only abdominal pain (GT) was more frequent in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm (14% vs. 10%), upper respiratory tract infection (GT) (15%) and lipase increased (12%) were similar in both arms. Grade 3 or 4 adverse events which were &gt;5\% in the asciminib arm were thrombocytopenia (GT) (13%) and neutropenia (GT) (10%).

L766: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L767: ## The Applicant's Position:
L768: Adverse drug reaction candidate screening was based primarily on the randomized period of Study J12301, whereas all available data from Studies J12301, A2301, and X2101 were considered for causality assessment and ADR frequency estimation.
L769: In Study J12301, the proportion of participants with at least one ADR was lower in the asciminib treatment group (85.5%) compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (93.5%) including 2G TKIs (97.1%) and comparable to imatinib (89.9%). The number of participants with at least one grade \ge 3 ADR was lower with asciminib (28.5%) compared to IS-TKIs (40.3%) including imatinib (33.3%) and 2G TKIs (47.1%) [CO-Section 5.1.7] (Table 249).
L770: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L771: Most of the TEAEs which are listed in Table 24 as adverse drug reactions (ADRs) are not considered as ADRs by the FDA. The Agency will consider any TEAEs with \ge 10\% incidence and occurring \ge 2\% more frequent on the treatment arm an ADR. Causality is difficult to assess in a trial with an active control arm. FDA used custom queries and PTs to test for ADRs in study CABL001J12301. The only TEAE which should be listed as an ADR is abdominal pain (GT) (14% in asciminib arm and 10% in IS-TKI arm). The differences in the evaluation of ADRs are not thought to influence the safety profile of asciminib.

L785: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L786: ## The Applicant's Position:
L787: ## **Hematology laboratory abnormalities**
L788: In **Study J12301**, worst post-baseline hematological laboratory abnormalities occurred in lower proportions of participants with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs including imatinib and 2G TKIs. Most of these hematologic abnormalities were mild-to-moderate (grade 1 or 2) in all treatment groups (asciminib, IS-TKI, imatinib and 2G TKI). These hematology results are consistent with the observed hematologic AE profile for the study drugs (Table 2520) [SCS-Section 3.1].
L789: There were no new or worsened post-baseline hematological laboratory abnormalities that were reported more frequently (and with a \ge 5\% difference) with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs including imatinib and 2G TKIs [CO-Section 5.1.4].
L790: ## **Biochemistry laboratory abnormalities**
L791: Most of the biochemistry abnormalities were mild-to-moderate (grade 1 or 2) in all treatment groups. Few notable differences in the post-baseline biochemistry laboratory abnormalities were observed in the asciminib group vs. IS-TKI group including imatinib and 2G TKIs (Table 261).
L792: Shifts from baseline normal to post-baseline high levels for serum glucose were observed in a lower proportion of participants in the asciminib treatment group (26.5%) compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (39.5%), including imatinib (36.7%) and 2G TKI (42.3%) groups. Of note, shifts from baseline normal to post-baseline high levels for serum HbA1C was observed in a fewer proportion of participants (2.9% in asciminib group, 1.7 % in IS-TKI group including 0% in imatinib group, and 3.1% in 2G TKI group). There was no permanent discontinuation due to either blood glucose increases or hyperglycemia AEs [SCS-Section 3.2.1].
L793: ## **Liver enzymes**
L794: In Study J12301, increases in ALT or AST &gt;3\timesULN were noted in a comparatively lower number of participants in the asciminib treatment group compared to the IS-TKI treatment group. In the asciminib treatment group, a lower proportion of participants had total bilirubin increase of &gt;2\timesULN compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (1.5% vs. 4.5%), and particularly when compared to the 2G TKI group (7.8%). In both treatment groups, the majority of hepatic laboratory elevations (of AST and/or ALT and/or TBL) were noted as isolated increases. No concurrent elevations of ALT or AST &gt;3\timesULN and total bilirubin &gt;2\timesULN and ALP &lt;2\timesULN meeting Hy's law biochemical criteria were observed in either treatment group [SCS-Section 3.2.2].
L795: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L796: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of hematology parameters in study CABL001J12301. Myelosuppression resulting in anemia, leukopenia, and thrombocytopenia are known direct effects of tyrosine kinase inhibitors. Thrombocytopenia was the second most common cause for discontinuation of asciminib. Thrombocytopenia and neutropenia were the most common causes for dose reduction and dose interruption of asciminib. In study CABL001J12301, all grades and grades 3-4 anemia, leukopenia, neutropenia, lymphopenia, and
L797: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L798: thrombocytopenia were less frequent in the asciminib arm compared to the IS-TKI arm. Grades 3-4 thrombocytopenia was present in 12.5% of patients in the asciminib arm vs. 14.7% in the IS-TKI arm. Grade 3-4 neutropenia was seen in 12.5% of patients in the asciminib arm vs. 19.4% of those in the IS-TKI arm.
L799: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of biochemistry parameters in study CABL001J12301. All grades biochemical abnormalities, except cholesterol increase and potassium increase, were less frequent with asciminib compared to the IS-TKIs. Grades 3-4 biochemical abnormalities were not frequent in both arms. The most frequent grade 3-4 biochemical abnormality in the asciminib arm was lipase increase (9.5%) which was present at the same percentage in the IS-TKI arm. Other biochemical abnormalities which are not included in Table 26 include glucose increase (26% vs. 34%) (calculated by change of baseline low or normal values to post-baseline high values), GGT increase (29% vs. 37%), and creatine kinase increase (17% vs. 33%). There were no grade 3-4 glucose increases. Grade 3-4 GGT increase and creatine kinase increase were infrequent.
L800: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of hematology parameters in study CABL001AUS08 as presented in the CSR at IA2. The most frequent hematology abnormality was lymphocyte decrease (64.8%) followed by hemoglobin decrease (38%). The most frequent grade 3-4 hematology abnormality was neutrophil decrease (5.6%).
L801: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of biochemistry parameters in study CABL001AUS01 as presented in the CSR at IA2. The most frequent biochemistry abnormality was triglycerides increase (61%), followed by ALP increase (21%), amylase increase (16%), ALT increase (13%), and creatine kinase increase (13%).
L802: In general, hematology and biochemistry abnormalities in study CABL001J12301 and study CABL001AUS08 were similar.
L803: ## Vital Signs
L804: ## The Applicant's Position:
L805: In **Study J12301**, a higher proportion of participants in the asciminib treatment group reported increased systolic blood pressure (SBP) compared to IS-TKIs; \ge 140 mmHg (27.5% vs. 20.9%), \ge 160 mmHg (13.5% vs. 5.5%), and \ge 180 mmHg (1.5% vs. 0%) in line with a higher incidence of vascular disorders, particularly hypertension, previously reported with asciminib compared to IS-TKI treated participants. There were no meaningful differences between the two treatment groups for other vital signs assessed [SCS-Section 4.1].
L806: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L807: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L808: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs) and QT
L809: ## The Applicant's Position:
L810: In Study J12301, the proportion of participants with notable ECG intervals (including QTcF, QT, QRS, and PR) was low in all treatment groups, with a few notable differences observed when comparing the asciminib treatment group to the IS-TKI treatment group including imatinib and 2G TKIs. QTcF increase from baseline &gt; 30 to ≤ 60 ms occurred in a lower proportion of participants treated with asciminib (2.0%) compared to IS-TKIs (4.5%; imatinib 4.0%, and 2G TKIs 4.9%). Post-baseline QTcF &gt; 450 to ≤ 480 ms also occurred in a lower proportion of participants treated with asciminib (2.5%) compared to IS-TKIs (7.1%), as well as imatinib (5.1%) and 2G TKIs (9.2%). None of the participants in the asciminib and 2G TKI treatment groups as compared to 2 participants (2.0%) treated with imatinib experienced a QTcF &gt; 480 to ≤ 500 ms.
L811: Similarly, post-baseline uncorrected QT interval increase from baseline &gt; 30 to ≤ 60 ms occurred in a lower proportion of participants with asciminib (24.5%) compared to IS-TKIs (36.8%), including as compared to imatinib (35.4%) and 2G TKIs (38.2%) [SCS-Section 4.2].
L812: QTc prolongation is further discussed in Section 8.2.5.
L813: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L814: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L815: ## Immunogenicity
L816: The Applicant's Position: Not applicable as this was not assessed nor expected.
L817: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L818: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L832: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L833: ## The Applicant's Position:
L834: The AESIs were defined based on the class effects of other TKIs in the same class, mechanism of action and the current pre-clinical and clinical knowledge of the study drug. Compared to the original submission in Ph+ CML-CP, arterial-occlusive events (AOEs) have been added as an AESI for asciminib, (b) (4)
L835: An overview of AESIs is presented in Table 27. The overall pattern of AESIs associated with asciminib was consistent with its currently known safety profile and with that of the All Asciminib Safety Pool (Studies J12301, A2301, X2101). No new or worsening safety concerns were observed with asciminib in Study J12301 and in the All Asciminib Safety Pool.
L836: Key categories of events related to myelosuppression, hypersensitivity, hepatotoxicity (including laboratory terms), hepatotoxicity (clinical events), acute pancreatitis (including isolated pancreatic enzyme elevations), pancreatic toxicity (clinical events), arterial-occlusive events (AOEs), and QTc prolongation, are discussed below, other AESI are discussed and summarized in [SCS-Section 2.6]. In Study J12301, there were no cases of hepatitis B virus reactivation [SCS-Section 2.6].
L837: Overall, fewer AESIs, including TKI-class effects or other safety risks, were observed in participants treated with asciminib, in contrast to participants treated with IS-TKIs, including imatinib and 2G TKIs. The cumulative incidence of AESI events for most of the topics was lower with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs. For the AESI topics of acute pancreatitis and myelosuppression (thrombocytopenia), the cumulative incidence of events was comparable between the two treatment groups as expected [SCS-Section 2.6] [SCS Appendix 1-Figure 2.1-1].
L838: ## 8.2.5.1 Myelosuppression
L839: In Study J12301, myelosuppression events (all grades and grade \ge 3) occurred less frequently with asciminib (all grades: 43.0%; grade \ge 3: 19.0%) compared to IS-TKIs (all grades: 57.2%; grade \ge 3: 27.9%) including as compared to imatinib (all grades: 51.5%; grade \ge 3: 23.2%) and 2G TKI (all grades: 62.7%; grade \ge 3: 32.4%) (Table 2722). Grade 4 events occurred in similar proportions across all treatment groups. In most participants, these events were suspected to be related to study treatment, as assessed by the Investigator. SAEs occurred in a few participants across the treatment groups [CO-Section 5.1.3.1].
L840: In all treatment groups, these events were successfully managed by dose interruptions, dose reductions, and additional medications/therapies; treatment discontinuation due to myelosuppression events was low.
L841: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L842: ## 8.2.5.2 Hypersensitivity
L843: Hypersensitivity events (all grades) occurred markedly less frequently with asciminib (24.0%) as compared to IS-TKIs (41.3%) including as compared to imatinib (41.4%) and 2G TKIs (41.2%) (Table 2722).
L844: The proportion of participants with grade \ge 3 events was low in all treatment groups and no SAEs were reported in either treatment group. Treatment interruption (asciminib: 1.0%, IS-TKIs: 3.5%, imatinib: 3.0%, 2G TKI: 3.9%), dose adjustment (asciminib: 0%, IS-TKIs: 3.0%, imatinib: 2.0%, 2G TKI: 3.9%), and discontinuation (asciminib: 0%, IS-TKIs: 2.0%, imatinib: 1.0%, 2G TKI: 2.9%) due to hypersensitivity events was less frequent with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs including as compared to imatinib and 2G TKIs [CO-Section 5.1.3.2].
L845: ## 8.2.5.3 Hepatotoxicity (including laboratory terms and clinical events)
L846: Hepatotoxicity events (including laboratory values) (all grades and grade \ge 3 events) occurred less frequently with asciminib (all grades: 16.5%; grade \ge 3: 2.5%) as compared to IS-TKIs (all grades: 26.9%; grade \ge 3: 6.0%) including as compared to 2G TKI (all grades: 34.3%; grade \ge 3: 7.8%) and was marginally lower to imatinib group (all grades: 19.2%; grade \ge 3: 4.0%) (Table 2722).
L847: In most participants, these events were suspected to be related to study treatment, as assessed by the Investigator and were reversible, and not considered to be clinically relevant. Hepatotoxicity events leading to study treatment dose adjustment occurred less frequently across all treatment groups. The proportion of participants with hepatotoxicity (including laboratory terms) SAEs were low in all treatment groups [CO-Section 5.1.3.3].
L848: Hepatotoxicity clinical events (all grades and grade \ge 3 events) were comparable between the asciminib (all grades: 1.5%, grade \ge 3: 0.5%) and IS-TKI treatment group (all grades: 2.0%, grade \ge 3: 0.5%), including the imatinib (1% for both all grades and grade \ge 3) and 2G TKI treatment groups (all grades: 2.9%, grade \ge 3: 0%) (Table 2722).
L849: The incidence of PTs related to hepatotoxicity clinical events was low across the treatment groups. Hepatotoxicity clinical events leading to study treatment dose adjustment and dose interruption occurred in 1.0% of participants each in IS-TKI treatment group compared to none in the asciminib treatment group. The proportion of participants with hepatotoxicity (clinical events) SAEs were low in all treatment groups. Grade 4 hepatotoxicity event (not treatment-related, resolved) occurred in one participant in the asciminib treatment group and none in IS-TKI group [CO-Section 5.1.3.3].
L850: ## 8.2.5.4 Acute pancreatitis and pancreatic toxicity
L851: Acute pancreatitis events, including isolated pancreatic enzyme elevations, (all grades and grade \ge 3) occurred at a similar frequency with asciminib and IS-TKIs (all grades: 13.0% vs. 15.4%; grade \ge 3: 3.5% vs. 3.5%), as well as imatinib (all grades: 16.2%, grade \ge 3: 2.0%) and 2G TKIs (all grades:
L852: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L853: 14.7%; grade \ge3: 4.9%) (Table 2722). The majority of events were enzyme elevations without clinical manifestations.
L854: Lipase increased and amylase increased were the most frequently reported acute pancreatitis/pancreatic toxicity events. The occurrences of all individual acute pancreatitis events were comparable across all treatment groups. Grade 4 events occurred in 2 participants (lipase increase) with asciminib and in 1 participant (amylase increased) with IS-TKIs (2G TKI).
L855: The proportion of participants with grade \ge3 events, events leading to study treatment dose adjustment, interruption or withdrawal, and SAEs were low in all treatment groups and comparable. There were 3 treatment discontinuations reported with asciminib compared to 1 with IS-TKIs, specifically in imatinib group [CO-Section 5.1.3.4].
L856: The incidence of pancreatic toxicity clinical events was low and comparable in both the treatment groups (1%) (asciminib and IS-TKI). In asciminib treatment group, these events were reported in 1 participant each, pancreatitis (serious, grade 3, suspected to be treatment-related, was resolved with drug interruption) and pancreatitis acute (serious, grade 3, suspected to be treatment-related, resolved). In the IS-TKI group, pancreatic toxicity clinical events were reported in 2 participants, pancreatitis acute (nonserious, grade 2, not treatment-related, resolved) in 1 participant, and pancreatitis acute (serious, grade 3, not treatment-related, resolved) and pancreatitis (serious, grade 3, suspected to be treatment-related, improved to grade 2, ongoing at data cut-off, drug was eventually discontinued) in the other participant [CO-Section 5.1.3.4].
L857: ## 8.2.5.5 QTc prolongation
L858: The incidence of QTc prolongation events were comparable between asciminib and IS-TKI treatment groups (1.5% vs. 2.0%). The frequency of these events was lower in asciminib compared to 2G TKI treated participants (1.5% vs. 3.9%). None of the imatinib treated participants had QTc prolongation events (Table 2722).
L859: None of the electrocardiogram QTc prolonged events were considered to be SAEs in both the treatment groups except for one 2G TKI treated participant in IS-TKI treatment group, and none were of grade 4 or grade 5 in intensity in either of the treatment groups. The grade 3 events were syncope and ECG QT prolonged in 1 participant each in the asciminib treatment group [CO-Section 5.1.3.6].
L860: ## 8.2.5.6 Arterial-occlusive events
L861: Arterial-occlusive events occurred in 2 (1.0%) participants treated with asciminib and 2 (2.0%) participants treated with 2G TKIs. None of the imatinib treated participants had an AOE (Table 2722). In the asciminib treatment group, one participant had an SAE of cerebrovascular accident (grade 4, suspected to be treatment-related, resolved in one day). In the 2G TKI treatment group, one participant had an SAE of myocardial infarction (grade 4, suspected to be treatment-related by the Investigator, resolved) [CO-Section 5.1.3.5].
L862: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L863: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L864: FDA performed analysis of all GTs including AESI in study CABL001J12301 and in the Asciminib Safety Pool of 556 patients. The Warnings and Precautions of the label includes myelosuppression, pancreatic toxicity, hypertension, hypersensitivity, and cardiovascular toxicity in 556 patients. Myelosuppression is the most frequent AESI in the CABL001J12301 study. Pancreatic toxicity manifests itself as pancreatic enzyme (amylase, lipase) elevations and pancreatitis. Hypertension is a known adverse event with asciminib and the USPI recommends monitoring for hypertension and using standard antihypertensive therapy, if needed. Hypersensitivity is another common risk factor with the TKIs. Cardiovascular toxicity section of the USPI consists of three main ADRs which are cardiovascular toxicity (including ischemic cardiac and CNS conditions, arterial thrombotic and embolic conditions), cardiac failure, and arrhythmia (including QTc prolongation).
L865: FDA agrees with the Applicant's general assessment of submission-specific issues. In study CABL001J12301, AESI which were \ge 10\% less frequent with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs were myelosuppression (GT) (43% vs. 56%) and hypersensitivity (GT) (23% vs. 41%).
L866: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of myelosuppression (GT). The most frequent form of myelosuppression is thrombocytopenia (28%), followed by neutropenia (25%), and anemia (12%). Myelosuppression (GT), thrombocytopenia (GT), neutropenia (GT), and anemia (GT) were less frequent with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs.
L867: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of pancreatic toxicity (GT). Pancreatic toxicity (GT) was observed in similar frequencies in the asciminib arm vs the IS-TKI arm (13% vs. 15%). The proposed USPI was referring to elevation of serum lipase and amylase as "asymptomatic" and the Applicant was requested to remove the word "asymptomatic" because these were reported as an elevation not relating to symptoms, and it was thought that the word "asymptomatic" understated the clinical significance of these elevations.
L868: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of hypersensitivity (GT). The most frequent hypersensitivity event in the asciminib arm of CABL001J12301 was rash (13% vs. 16%).
L869: In study CABL001J12301, cardiovascular toxicity (GT) was seen in 4.5% of patients in the asciminib arm and 5.5% of patients in the IS-TKI arm. In the Asciminib Safety Pool of 556 patients in the USPI, cardiovascular toxicity (GT) was reported in 11% patients. Cardiac failure was not present in any patients in study CABL001J12301, and it was reported in 2.3% patients in the Asciminib Safety Pool. Arrhythmia (GT) occurred in 2% of patients in CABL001J12301 and in 6% in the Asciminib Safety Pool. L877: Other AESI which were \ge 10\% less frequent with asciminib but not included in the USPI are gastrointestinal toxicity (GT) (34% vs. 45%), hepatotoxicity (GT) (16% vs. 26%), and edema (3.5% vs. 20%). L888: ## **8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups**
L889: ## The Applicant's Position:
L890: Subgroup analyses were conducted to identify potential safety issues that might have been limited to a subgroup of participants, or safety issues that were more commonly observed in a subgroup of participants [CO-Section 5.1.5.1].
L891: The following subgroup analyses were performed for Study J12301 and the All Asciminib Safety Pool:
L892: - Age group (18-&lt; 65, ≥65, ≥75 years)
- Gender (male vs. female)
- Race (Caucasian, Asian, Other)
- Renal impairment at baseline (moderate, mild, normal)
- Hepatic impairment at baseline (moderate, mild, normal)
L893: In Study J12301, exposure (measured in PTY) across all age groups (18 to &lt; 65 years, ≥65 years, and ≥75 years) irrespective of gender and race was comparable between both asciminib and IS-TKI treatment groups.
L894: ## **Overview of AEs by age, gender, and race (Study J12301)**
L895: **Age:** Overall, no meaningful differences in the safety of asciminib were observed between participants 65 years of age or older compared to younger participants. AEs and treatment-related AEs were comparable between all age categories across all treatment groups other than as expected, a higher incidence of AEs was observed in participants ≥75 years compared to those &lt;75 years. However, no imbalance in the type of events or age-related events was observed, with asciminib showing a consistent safety profile across all age groups [SCS-Section 5.1.1.2].
L896: Most of the AESIs were consistent across all age groups in both the treatment groups except for: Acute pancreatitis events occurred at a higher incidence in participants aged ≥65 years across
L897: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L898: the treatment groups compared to 18 to &lt;65 years however, in participants ≥75 years, these events occurred at a lower incidence with asciminib compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (10.0% vs. 21.4%); edema and fluid retention events in asciminib treatment group occurred at a higher incidence in ≥75 years age group (20.0%) compared to the 18 to &lt;65 years (2.6%) and ≥65 years (6.5%) age groups; these events however were less frequent with asciminib compared to IS-TKIs in ≥65 years age group (6.5% vs. 29.2%). Most of the AESIs in asciminib treatment group occurred at lower incidence compared to the IS-TKI treatment group across all age group categories except for hemorrhage events which occurred at a higher incidence in participants ≥75 years in asciminib treatment group compared to the IS-TKI treatment group (20.0% vs. 0%) [SCS-Section 5.1.1.2].
L899: **Gender:** Overall, no clinically meaningful differences in safety profile of asciminib were observed by gender [SCS-Section 5.1.1.3].
L900: **Race:** In the asciminib treatment group, the incidence of most of the AE categories was similar in White and Asian subgroups. In the IS-TKI treatment group, the incidence of AEs leading to dose adjustment/interruption (40.7% vs. 52.8%) and AEs requiring additional therapy (78.7% vs. 89.9%) was higher in Asian participants compared to White/Caucasian participants [SCS-Section 5.1.1.4].
L901: ## **Overview of AEs based on renal and hepatic impairment status at baseline**
L902: In **Study J12301**, based on the eGFR at baseline, the number of participants in the asciminib and IS-TKI treatment group, respectively, with normal renal function were 119 vs. 123 participants and 68 vs. 67 participants with mild renal impairment. As the number of participants with moderate renal impairment is small in both the treatment groups (13 and 11 participants), this group has not been considered for comparison.
L903: In both the treatment groups, the incidence of most of the AEs across all the AE categories was similar in the mild renal impairment and normal renal function group and was comparable to the incidence of AEs observed in the overall patient population in Study J12301.
L904: Most of the AESIs were consistent in participants with mild renal impairment and participants with normal renal function except for: Gastrointestinal toxicity (asciminib treatment group) higher incidence in mild renal impairment vs. normal renal function subgroup (41.2 % vs. 28.6%); myelosuppression (asciminib treatment group) lower incidence in mild renal impairment vs. normal renal function subgroup (36.8% vs. 47.1%); hypersensitivity (imatinib treated participants) higher incidence in mild renal impairment vs. normal renal function subgroup (50.0% vs. 34.5%); acute pancreatitis events higher incidence in mild renal impairment vs. normal renal function subgroup in the asciminib treatment group (19.1% vs. 8.4%) and vice-versa for imatinib treated participants (13.2% vs. 20.0%); edema and fluid retention higher incidence in mild renal impairment vs. normal renal function subgroup in IS TKI treatment group (17.9% vs. 9.8%), including for imatinib (18.4% vs. 9.1%).
L905: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L906: Except for myelosuppression events, none of the grade \ge 3 AESIs occurred at a higher incidence (\ge 10\%) across all subgroups in both the treatment groups. The incidence of most of the grade \ge 3 AESIs in both the asciminib and IS-TKI treatment groups was comparable between the mild renal impairment and normal subgroups [SCS-Section 5.1.1.5].
L907: In **Study J12301**, as most participants in this subgroup analysis had normal hepatic function at baseline (357 participants) with a small number of participants with mild hepatic impairment (43 participants), a meaningful comparison cannot be made across various AEs categories and the results were comparable to the overall analysis of AEs in Study J12301 [SCS-Section 5.1.1.6]. Subgroup analysis in All Asciminib Safety Pool is presented in the [SCS-Section 5.1.1]
L908: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L909: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. In the Asciminib Safety Pool of 556 patients, TEAEs \ge 10\% were similar in patients &lt;65 vs. \ge 65 years of age, and in patients &lt;75 vs. \ge 75 years of age. Two TEAEs which were higher in patients \ge 75 years compared to patients &lt;75 years were musculoskeletal pain (GT) (52% vs. 35%) and fatigue (GT) (32% vs. 25%). The higher frequency of these two TEAEs may be related to older age and concomitant diseases. In study CABL001J12301, TEAEs in patients &lt;65 and \ge 65 years of age were similar except neutropenia (28% vs. 15%), hypersensitivity (25% vs. 13%), and rash (21% vs. 9%) which were \ge 10\% higher in patients &lt;65 years of age. In study CABL001J12301, musculoskeletal pain (GT) (22% vs. 70%) was much higher in patients \ge 75 years of age compared to those aged &lt;75 years.
L910: Female and male patients compared similarly in terms of TEAEs. When White patients were compared to Asian patients, it was seen that there was \ge 10\% difference in the TEAEs of musculoskeletal pain (GT) (31% vs. 18%), myelosuppression (GT) (31% vs. 59%), fatigue (GT) (28% vs. 7%), and headache (GT) (18% vs. 8%) between the groups. The number of Black or African American patients was not sufficient to include in racial comparisons.
L911: ## 8.2.8. **Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials**
L912: ## The Applicant's Position:
L913: Study AUS08 showed a consistent and favorable safety profile in 43 participants with Ph+ CML-CP in the 2L cohort in the Safety Set at 6 months. There were no major safety concerns observed. The median duration of exposure to study treatment was 11.4 weeks (range: 0-66). One participant had SAEs (chest pain, catheter site pain, and intractable nausea and vomiting), all were grade 3 events, and while nausea was reported as related to asciminib, vomiting, catheter site pain and chest pain were reported as not related to asciminib. All SAEs were reported to have been resolved. One participant had AEs leading to treatment discontinuation (nausea, grade 3, treatment-related). There were no deaths [CO-Section 5.2].

L915: ## Scemblix (asciminib)

L917: Twenty-eight patients completed the 6-month efficacy assessment or discontinued treatment prior in the 2L cohort. Seventy-one patients were enrolled in the 2L cohort between 11 November 2022 and 28 June 2024 and were included in the Full Analysis Set. Safety results of 71 patients in the 2L Asciminib cohort related to study CABL001AUS08 are included in relevant sections of the Assessment Aid.
L918: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L919: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L920: ## The Applicant’s Position:
L921: Based on the currently available nonclinical data from the 2-year rat carcinogenicity study, the relevance of these findings to humans is unknown. Based on routine pharmacovigilance activities to date and the evaluation of cumulative data available in the Clinical Database and Novartis Safety Database, there is no evidence of a potential safety signal of any ovarian malignant or benign tumors in asciminib-treated patients.
L922: ## The FDA’s Assessment:
L923: FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. On 6 February 2024 the Applicant submitted Supplement 007 which included the results of a 2-year carcinogenicity study of asciminib in Wistar Han rats and it was based on data from a 26-week repeat-dose toxicology. There was a statistically significant increase in the incidence of benign Sertoli cell tumors in the ovaries of high-dose female rats. Currently, the clinical significance of these findings in humans is unknown. Relevant changes were incorporated into section 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility of the USPI. Supplement 007 was approved on 6 August 2024. Refer to the nonclinical pharmacology toxicology review.
