L942: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L943: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L944: ## The Applicant's Position:
L945: Up to 15-Jan-2024, no new safety signals were identified based on regular reviews by qualified medical personnel of worldwide literature searches and frequency analyses in external databases or registries containing safety data as part of routine signal detection activities. No new safety signals and no substantial changes to the safety profile of the drug were identified for the reviews conducted for SAEs and deaths from ongoing studies whose data were included in the pooled analyses, for events reported between the latest cut-off date for the pooled data (28-Nov-2023 for Study J12301) and 15-Jan-2024. Furthermore, no new or changing safety signal was identified for the reporting interval from 29-Apr-2023 to 28-Oct-2023 of the most recent PSUR presenting postmarketing analyses with an estimated cumulative post-marketing exposure of (b) (4) patient-treatment years [SCS-Section 6].

L954: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L955: ## The Applicant's Position:
L956: The results from the registration study J12301 in participants with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP shows that the safety data is consistent with the known safety profile of asciminib. These results were further strengthened by the results from the All Asciminib Safety Pool where longer-term treatment continued to be consistent with the previously established safety profile. An unmet need related to safety for treatment of 1L Ph+ CML-CP is a specific targeted therapy with improved safety/tolerability profile. Asciminib shows favorable safety and tolerability vs. Investigator selected-TKIs overall, including vs. 2G TKIs and vs. imatinib, fulfilling this unmet need in the newly diagnosed patient population.
L957: ## Key conclusions
L958: - No new or worsening safety concerns were identified from the data from Study J12301, further reinforcing the safety profile of asciminib.
- In Study J12301, overall, AEs occurred in 93.5% of participants treated with asciminib compared to 93.9% participants treated with imatinib and 100% participants treated with 2G TKIs (97.0% for IS-TKIs overall).
- All other key categories of AEs were less frequently observed in asciminib-treated participants compared to participants treated with IS-TKI (imatinib or 2G TKIs), irrespective of whether considered related to study treatment as assessed by the Investigator, including: severe AEs (grade \ge3 AEs), serious AEs (SAEs), AEs leading to discontinuation, AEs leading to dose interruption/dose adjustment, and AEs requiring additional therapy.
L959: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L960: - Asciminib has a better safety profile for most of the safety topics including those which are considered as class effects for TKIs. In Study J12301 overall, AESIs were reported in a lower proportion of participants treated with asciminib. The proportion of participants experiencing treatment-emergent AESIs of myelosuppression, gastrointestinal toxicity, hepatotoxicity (including laboratory terms), hypersensitivity, and fluid retention/edema were lower in the asciminib treatment group compared to the IS-TKI treatment group, including imatinib and 2G TKI treated participants (both all grades and grade \ge 3). There was no cardiac failure reported in asciminib and imatinib-treated participants; 2 dasatinib-treated participants had severe cardiac failure (grade \ge 3).
- The safety profile for age, race, gender, and renal and hepatic impairment subgroups was consistent with the overall safety profile observed in participants with newly-diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP treated with asciminib in Study J12301.
- Size of the safety database and duration of exposure were considered robust and adequate for safety assessment of asciminib in clinical studies (n=556 All Asciminib Safety Pool), with additional postmarketing data of (b) (4) patient-treatment years as of 28-Oct-2023, and with a maximum treatment duration of 8 years and median duration of treatment of 6 years from Study X2101.
- The data from the All Asciminib Safety Pool further strengthen the safety of asciminib with longer exposure and demonstrate consistency with the known safety profile of asciminib.
L961: ## **Overall Summary**
L962: Asciminib 80 mg QD in Study J12301 demonstrated a markedly favorable safety and tolerability profile with fewer all grade and severe (grade \ge 3) AEs, SAEs, and AEs requiring dose interruption/dose adjustments or treatment discontinuations, all grades and severe (grade \ge 3) AESIs, compared to IS-TKI overall as well as compared to participants treated with imatinib and 2G TKIs. In particular, asciminib demonstrated a more favorable safety profile across all key safety parameters, including TKI class risks, vs. 2G TKIs.
L963: Thus, the safety of asciminib can be established by extrapolation to the 2L setting based on the existing safety profile in 3L+, the absence of any new safety concerns in 1L, and the fact that this 2L setting does not have any unique disease characteristics that would impact the safety profile. Emerging safety data from Study AUS08 showed that the safety data is consistent with the previously established safety profile of asciminib as of the data cut-off of 22-March-2024 with a median duration of exposure of 11.4 weeks in 43 participants. Only 1 participant had discontinued due to AE, and SAE was reported in 1 participant. Moreover, with asciminib also showing a better safety and tolerability profile in the 1L and 3L+ setting, compared to all IS-TKIs, including 2G TKIs which are most commonly used in the 2L setting, it can be expected that asciminib also shows better safety than 2G TKIs in the 2L setting.
L964: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L965: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L966: FDA agrees with the Applicant's overall safety summary. In study CABL001J12301, the most frequent TEAEs in the asciminib arm were thrombocytopenia (GT) (28%), neutropenia (GT) (25%), and musculoskeletal pain (GT) (25%). The most frequent grade 3-4 TEAEs were thrombocytopenia (GT) (13%) and neutropenia (GT) (10%). The most frequent TEAEs in the IS-TKI arm were similar to the asciminib arm, albeit at a higher frequency, neutropenia (GT) (33%), musculoskeletal pain (GT) (32%), and thrombocytopenia (GT) (31%). Most TEAEs \ge10% were less frequent in the asciminib arm than in the IS-TKI arm. The only TEAE which was more frequent with asciminib was abdominal pain (GT) (14% vs. 10%). Upper respiratory tract infection (GT) and lipase increased were similar in both arms (15% and 12%).
L967: Safety data from study CABL001AUS08 was evaluated to investigate safety in the 2L indication. In the safety set of 71 patients, musculoskeletal pain (GT) (27%) was the most frequent TEAE followed by vomiting (GT) (17%) and fatigue (16%). Grade 3-4 TEAEs in study CABL001AUS08 were infrequent. AESI \ge10% were hypertension (GT) (17%), hypersensitivity (GT) (16%), pancreatic toxicity (GT) (13%), and rash (GT) (10%).

L982: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L983: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L984: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L985: The statistical review team agrees with the Applicant that statistical aspects of study CABL001J12301 provide evidence for the effectiveness of the asciminib in newly-diagnosed Ph+ CP-CML. Asciminib demonstrated an improvement of 18.88% in MMR rate at Week 48 vs. the control arm. This endpoint is appropriate for this setting.
L986: The statistical review team notes that the overall treatment effect on MMR at Week 48 is not representative of the relative efficacy of asciminib versus all treatments used in the control arm. For instance, this difference was 8.17% for the 2nd generation TKI stratum. This heterogeneity was expected a priori, as all of the 2nd generation TKIs included were approved based on an improvement in response rates as compared to imatinib in randomized trials. Given this heterogeneity of treatment effect, the associated subgroup estimates should be considered more reliable for understanding the benefit of asciminib over imatinib or 2nd generation TKIs. This heterogeneity has no impact on the approvability of asciminib in the first-line setting.
L987: The MMR rates observed in study CABL001J12301 are higher than those reported historically. This speaks to the importance of randomization, as historical rates may not be representative of the efficacy of any one product. While not an issue for study CABL001J12301, study CABL001AUS08 was a single-arm trial whose interpretation is limited by the absence of a control arm as well as a limited sample size.
L988: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L989: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L990: The review team was able to verify or clarify the safety and efficacy endpoints as provided by the Applicant. The review team recommends approval of the NDA.
L991: The FDA approach toward approval in newly diagnosed CP-CML is to grant accelerated approval when there is at least 48 weeks (one year) of safety and efficacy data. For conversion to regular approval, the FDA requires 60 months (5 years) of safety and efficacy information. Follow up to the later time point is required only from the study-drug treatment arm in the case of an active-controlled study to demonstrate benefit with the use of the study drug. In this submission, the Applicant provided safety and efficacy information from study CABL001J12301 to 48 weeks. Therefore, the data from CABL001J12301 supports accelerated approval in patients with newly diagnosed Ph+ CP-CML. A PMR will be issued to obtain safety and efficacy information at 60 months (5 years).
L992: Asciminib has regular approval for the treatment of patients with Ph+ CP-CML, previously
L993: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L994: treated with two or more TKIs. They have provided 6-month efficacy data from study CABL001AUS08 when a minimum of 28 patients in the 2L cohort had completed their week 24 (6 months) visit or discontinued prior. The safety set includes 71 patients enrolled in the 2L cohort. As the safety and efficacy data from study CABL001AUS08 was found acceptable and adequate safety and efficacy information was provided from studies A2301 and J12301, the “previously treated with two or more TKIs” indication was updated to “previously treated” indication, and the review team recommends for this indication to be approved under regular approval.

L1051: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1052: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1053: FDA will request a PMR for 60 months (5 years) of follow-up data from the CABL001J12301 trial. The findings will be used to determine whether asciminib will receive regular approval for the newly diagnosed Ph+ CP-CML indication.
L1054: Current trials have not enrolled patients that are reflective of the ethnic diversity of patients with CML in the US. Black or African American and Hispanic / Latino patients with CML are underrepresented in study CABL001J12301. However, differential response and tolerance may exist among different racial and ethnic minorities. Therefore, FDA will request a PMC to determine whether asciminib shows a similar safety and efficacy profile in these underrepresented racial and ethnic groups.
L1055: Refer to FDA action letter for final PMR/PMC wording.
L1056: ## FDA PMC/PMR Checklist for Trial Diversity and U.S. Population Representativeness

L1138: ## 19.4.1. **Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance**
L1139: The Applicant's summary of the validation data supporting the bioanalytical methods used for determination of asciminib concentrations in human plasma samples from Study CABL001J12301 was provided in a response to information request and is provided below. FDA assessment is provided in the tables highlighted in gray.
L1140: Summary method DMPK R1701209 performance of a bioanalytical method to measure asciminib (ABL001) in Human plasma
L1141: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Method # 1 Report Name: Method Validation Report
Report Title: Quantitative determination of ABL001 in human plasma by LC-MS/MS
Amendments: 01, 02, 03 and 04
[DMPK R1701209] (original), [DMPK R1701209-01] (amendment 1), [DMPK R1701209-02] (amendment 2), [DMPK R1701209-03] (amendment 3), [DMPK R1701209-04] (amendment 4)
Method description	Quantitative determination of ABL001 in human plasma by LC-MS/MS
50 µL plasma (blanks, zeros, standards, QCs or unknowns) was added to polypropylene tube. A 50 µL aliquot of D5-ABL001 internal standard working solution (500 ng/mL in 50/50 (v/v) acetonitrile/water) was added to each tube except for the tubes for blanks, where a 50 µL aliquot of 50/50 (v/v) acetonitrile/water was added to each tube. A 300 µL aliquot of acetonitrile was added to each tube. The tubes were vortex-mixed, followed by centrifugation at approximately 4000 rpm for 10 minutes at 10±2°C. Supernatant was
L1142: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation sNDA 215358, S-008
L1143: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1144: transferred to pre-labeled autosampler vials. A 5 µL aliquot of the reconstituted extract was injected into the LC-MS/MS system.

L1171: ## 19.4.2. Population PK Analysis
L1172: ## 19.4.2.1. Executive Summary
L1173: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1174: The Applicant updated the population PK (PPK) analysis using asciminib dose and concentration data from Studies J12301 (CML, prior treatment=0, n=197), A2301 (CML, prior treatments = 1, 2 or NA, n=154), X2101 (CML, prior treatments = 1,2,3,4,5, or NA, n=240), A2013 (HV HI study,
L1175: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1176: n=32), and A2105 (HV RI study, n=14). The updated population PK modeling in the current submission did not identify any additional significant covariates on the PK of asciminib. The treatment line was not identified as a covariate of any PPK parameters in the Applicant's PPK analysis.
L1177: ## 19.4.2.2. PPK Assessment Summary
L1178: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1179: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	
To revise the previously developed PopPK model for oral administration of asciminib in 3L CML participants with additional data and re-evaluate covariates that were previously assessed to impact the PK of asciminib,
To determine whether asciminib PK differs between lines of therapy (1L vs. 3L+),
To evaluate the clinical relevance of covariates of interest through comparison of simulated exposures using population estimates,
To simulate individual daily exposure metrics (AUC_{0-24h}, Ctrough, Cmax) of asciminib using empirical Bayes estimates in participants from studies X2101, A2301, and J12301 using participants' actual dosing records, to support exposure-response analyses,
To use simulated exposure metrics for an updated PK profile comparison of the asciminib 40 mg BID and 80 mg QD dosing regimens.

Study Included	Studies J12301, A2301, X2101, A2103, and A2105
Doses Included	
Study J12301: 80 mg QD
Study A2301: 40 mg BID
Study X2101: (10 mg BID up to 200 mg QD) multiple doses including 80 mg QD
Studies A2103 and A2105: 40 mg single dose

Population Included	
The analysis was conducted on the following studies, referred to as the PKPD analysis set 1 (PKPDAS1, N=637):
Study J12301 (n=197): all 1L CML-CP participants in the asciminib arm
Study A2301 (n=154): all 3L CML-CP participants in the asciminib arm; participants who switched from bosutinib to asciminib were excluded
Study X2101 (n=240): all participants with CML who were treated with asciminib alone (including AP, CP, BP, Ph+ ALL, and participants with CML with the T315I mutation)
Study A2103 (n=32): all participants (healthy volunteers and participants with hepatic impairment)
L1180: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1181: Study A2105 (n=14): all participants (healthy volunteers and participants with renal impairment)
Note: Three participants from Study J12301, 2 participants from Study A2301 and 3 participants from Study X2101 had missing PK samples and were not included in the PopPK pool.

Population Characteristics	General	Pooled set (Studies A2103, A2105, X2101, A2301, J12301)
Age median (range): 54 (18-88) years
Weight median (range): 75.8 (34.9-184.2) kg
Male: 387 (60.8%)
Race: Asian 167 (26.2%), non-Asian 470 (73.8%)
Organ Impairment	Renal function classification by aGFR:
Normal: 348 (54.6%), Mild: 233 (36.6%), Moderate: 48 (7.5%), Severe: 8 (1.3%)
Hepatic function classification (NCI liver function criteria)
Normal: 540 (84.8%), Mild: 88 (13.8%), Moderate: 4 (0.6%), Severe: 5 (0.8%)

Sampling Schedule	Rich Sampling	Table 33
In ITT Population	Table 33
Covariates Evaluated	Static	Sex, baseline body weight, baseline age, race (Asian vs. non-Asian), asciminib nominal total daily dose, aGFR, formulation (capsule vs. FMI tablet), baseline renal function (normal vs. mild, moderate or severe), baseline hepatic function (normal vs. mild, moderate or severe), number of prior lines of TKI therapy (0-7), study effect, patients vs. healthy participants, patient population (CML-CP, CML-AP, CML-BP, Ph+ ALL, healthy participants).

Final Model	Summary	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
Software and Version	The analysis was performed using the Monolix software system, MONOLIX 2023 R2 (Lixoft, Paris, France) utilizing the DaVinci high performance computing environment accessed from GPSII. All model building was performed using Stochastic Approximation Expectation Maximization (SAEM) method.	Acceptable
L1182: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1183: Data preparation, graphical exploration, goodness-of-fits plots and other model diagnostics were performed using R 4.3.1.	
Model Structure	
A PopPK model is composed of (1) the structural model, which accounts for the systematic trends in the data and, to the extent possible, the mechanisms generating those trends; and (2) the random effects model, which accounts for the inter-individual variability about those trends.
PK structural model was a two-compartment disposition model, with first order absorption (K_a) with time lag (lag), clearance (CL), central and peripheral volume of distribution (V_1 and V_2), and inter-compartmental clearance (Q). With oral PK data, the V_1, Q, V_2 and CL here implicitly contain the 1/F term, wherein F refers to the relative bioavailability factor.
Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 34	Acceptable
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Relative standard errors of parameter estimates were less than 40% for the fixed effects and less than 10% for the random effects. Interindividual variability (IIV) for apparent clearance and volume of central compartment was less than 0.4 (SD). Larger IIV reported for lag, K_a and apparent peripheral volume.	Acceptable
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	Of the total number of PK samples, 4.0% of the sample concentrations were BLQ. M4 method, which maximized the log-likelihood for all the data by treating the BLQ observation as a censored endpoint, was implemented in the model.	Acceptable
GOF, VPC	There was good agreement between the observed data and the population fitted or individual fitted data for the final covariate model. The individual weighted residual (iwRes) versus time and population fit plots showed a horizontal line centered around 0 across time and increasing concentrations, respectively, indicating that there was no time-varying and concentration-dependent change in asciminib clearance. NPDE plots versus time and population fit also did not show any major trends [PopPK report-Figure 7-4, Figure 7-5, Figure 7-6].	Acceptable
L1184: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1185: Prediction-corrected visual predictive checks (pcVPC) showed that the model adequately captures the majority of the observed data, indicating that the final PK model described the observations reasonably well for the relevant doses and dosing regimens evaluated in the patient population [PopPK report-Figure 7-7].	
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	The covariates retained in the final model have been previously investigated either in the [Asciminib original popPK report] or in the FDA independent review, and results of these evaluations suggested their effects were deemed to not be clinically significant. Simulations based on the updated popPK model confirmed this assessment.	Acceptable
Analysis Based on Simulation (optional)	Overall, the mean AUC0-24h values were very similar between the two regimens (40 mg BID and 80 mg QD), while the mean Cmax and Cmin (concentration at the end of a dosing interval) of 80 mg QD were 1.55-fold and 0.69-fold of that of 40 mg BID, respectively (Table 35).	Acceptable
Labeling Language	Description	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
12.3 PK	As described in the approved prescribing information. No new information has been added based on this application.	Acceptable
Source: [PopPK report-Section 5, Section 6.5, Section 7.6.2, Section 7.7, Section 7.8; PopPK report-Table 7-2, Table 7-5, Table 7-6; PopPK report-Figure 7-4, Figure 7-5, Figure 7-6, Figure 7-7].

L1195: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1196: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1197: Overall, the updated PPK model is consistent with the PPK from the original NDA submission. We noted that the PK data of healthy volunteers from Study A2103 and A2105 appear to be different than that of patients. If only PK data of CML patients are included in the PPK analysis, TLAG1 is not needed in the structure model.
L1198: ## **19.4.2.3. PPK Review Issues (As mentioned in the last paragraph)**
L1199: ## **19.4.2.4. Reviewer's Independent Analysis (No need to include for no impact on labeling)**
L1200: ## **19.4.3. Exposure-Response Analysis**
L1201: ## **19.4.3.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary**
L1202: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1203: In the patients with newly diagnosed Ph+ CML-CP, the observed Week-48 MMR rate was 68% in Study J12301, and a positive ER relationship was observed as shown in Figure 7.
L1204: In addition, a 3-compartment model representing quiescent leukemic stem cells (q), proliferating bone marrow cells (p), and resistant cells (r) was developed using PKPD dataset from 430 participants with CML-CP to describe the time-course of BCR::ABL1 transcript. Drug kill of the p cells by asciminib was characterized by an Emax model. The potential influences of the number of previous TKI treatments (i.e. line of treatment) and patient's baseline and disease characteristics (including ELTS risk group) were investigated. The extrapolation of longitudinal BCR::ABL1 transcript response to asciminib in the 2L setting utilized the difference in the p, r and Emax parameters between nilotinib as first and second line treatments. As shown in Table 38, the predicted MMR rates were within those for both 1L and 3L+. For 2L, the prediction was also similar to the observation from Study AUS08. The PKPD model provided supportive evidence that asciminib is effective in patients with 2L Ph+ CML-CP.
L1205: ## **19.4.3.2. ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary**
L1206: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1207: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To describe the results of exposure-response analyses for efficacy to support the use of asciminib as treatment for newly diagnosed participants (first line, 1L) with Ph+ CML-CP, to determine the relationship between the exposure of asciminib and the time course of BCR-ABL% (IS, International Standard), and to extrapolate longitudinal BCR::ABL1 response in Ph+ CML-CP participants previously treated with one tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI, second line).
L1208: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1209: Study Included	Studies J12301, A2301, and X2101
Endpoint	Time course of BCR-ABL (%) (IS) for J12301, A2301 and X2101.

No. of Participants (total, and with individual PK)	PKPD dataset=430 participants with CML-CP, treated with asciminib.
Study X2101 (n=83): participants with CML-CP who were treated with asciminib alone.
Study A2301 (n=150): 3L participants with CML-CP in the asciminib arm; participants who switched from bosutinib to asciminib were excluded.

Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
Model Structure	A logistic regression analysis was used to analyze the exposure vs. binary MMR data at 48 week in J12301 study.
The semi-mechanistic PKPD model is a three-compartment model representing three subpopulations of leukemic cells in the bone marrow: 1) quiescent leukemic stem cells (q), 2) proliferating leukemic bone marrow cells (p), and	Acceptable
L1210: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1211: 3) proliferating resistant leukemic cells (r). Asciminib kills the p cells by an Emax term and reduces the net growth rate of p. The q cells are protected from drug effect in a peripheral compartment, whereas the r cells are impervious to asciminib.	
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 36	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	
Model adequacy was assessed by visual inspection on the standard goodness-of-fit plots:
Individual fit plots of observations, individual predictions, and population predictions (including covariate effects, if applicable) versus time,
Plots of individual weighted residuals versus individual predictions and versus time,
Relationships between empirical Bayes estimates and covariate to assess relevance of inclusion/exclusion of covariates from the model,
Visual predictive check (VPC) for the final model.
Acceptable
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
The result of the logistic regression showed positive relationship between exposure metrics (average daily AUC0-24 and Cmin) and the probability of MMR at Week 48 for asciminib 80 mg QD regimen based on the Binary MMR analysis set of Study J12301. Clopper-Pearsons confidence intervals showed lower MMR rates for the first quartile of exposure metrics and no correlation for second through the fourth quartiles of AUC0-24 and Cmin. The response was constant around 70% to 80% from the second to the fourth quartiles of AUC0-24 and Cmin, suggesting the response has reached plateau by second quartile of AUC0-24 and Cmin. Logistic regression model predictions follow the empirical probability estimates well.
The final PK-PD model retained number of treatment lines as covariate of p, r and Emax parameters; baseline log10-transformed BCR::ABL1 (L10BA0) was a covariate of p and r cell populations. The key covariate was "the number of treatment lines" which revealed important characteristics of drug-resistance associated with higher lines. The extrapolation into the 2L setting took advantage of this covariate effect by borrowing the information on the difference
Acceptable
L1212: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1213: between 1L and 2L setting on model parameters of the nilotinib PKPD model; the same total daily asciminib dose of 80 mg is expected to achieve satisfactory MMR rates with some degree of uncertainty.	
Simulation for Specific Population	Simulation of BCR-ABL (%) (IS) in 1L, 2L and 3L settings and computation of MMR rates at 48-, 96-weeks.	Acceptable
Visualization of E-R relationships	
The result of the logistic regression showed positive relationship between exposure metrics (average daily AUC0-24 and Cmin) and the probability of MMR at Week 48 for asciminib 80 mg QD regimen based on the Binary MMR analysis set of Study J12301. The first quartile exposure metrics were associated with lower MMR rate (~45%), as represented by red squares with error bars. Relatively more participants in the first quartile exposure had a relative dose intensity (RDI) ≤75% as represented by red dots, contributing to the low MMR rate (Figure 7).
Clopper-Pearsons confidence intervals showed lower MMR rates for the first quartile of exposure metrics and no correlation for second through the fourth quartiles of AUC0-24 and Cmin. The response was constant between 70% to 80% from the second to the fourth quartiles of AUC0-24 and Cmin, suggesting the response has reached plateau by second quartile of AUC0-24 and Cmin. Logistic regression model predictions follow the empirical probability estimates well.
Two scenarios were used in the extrapolation based on the line effect on the model parameters to provide a range of likely MMR rates in the 2L. These two scenarios were used to simulate the time-course of BCR::ABL1 to determine an MMR rate at Week 48 and Week 96 (Table 37).
In 200 in silico replicates of the original 430 participants using their recorded dosing schedule, a 48-week MMR rate of 70 ± 3% was predicted for the newly diagnosed CML-CP participants who were treated with asciminib 80 mg QD.
In 200 in silico replicates of 500 virtual participants per trial per number of lines, no dropout, dose reduction or interruption was assumed in the simulations to determine the MMR rates at Week 48 and Week 96. The simulations were based on the
Acceptable
L1214: ## Scemblix (asciminib)
L1215: average daily AUC0-24 generated from 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID. Assuming no dose interruption, the model predicted MMR rates at Weeks 48 and 96 were approximately 75% and 82%, respectively, in the 1L; the Week-48 MMR prediction is consistent with the results from the logistic regression analysis of binary MMR of the 2nd to 4th quartile exposure metrics.
For the extrapolation into 2L setting, the predicted MMR rates, assuming a total daily dose of 80 mg (80 mg QD or 40 mg BID) without interruption, ranged from 54 to 63% at Week 48 and 66 to 74% at Week 96. The lower MMR values were resulted from model parameter differences from the second scenario, whereas the higher values were from the first scenario. The predicted MMR rate in the 2L setting is in between the 1L and 3L settings (Table 38).

Goal of ER analysis	To assess the association between asciminib exposure and the probability of occurrence of key safety events using the pooled data from the newly diagnosed (1L) and pretreated (3L+) participants with Ph+ CML.

Study Included	Studies J12301, A2301, and X2101

Study X2101 (n= 199): all participants with CML who were treated with asciminib alone (including AP, CP, and participants with CML with the T315I mutation).

Endpoint	Laboratory parameters

L1269: The Applicant's ER analysis for safety is acceptable in general.
L1270: ## 19.4.3.4. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L1271: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1272: - The probability of occurrence of all safety events was lower for newly diagnosed than for pretreated participants with Ph+ CML. The higher event probability in pretreated participants is expected, given the persistent side effects of prior TKIs, increased incidence of cross-intolerance, and evolution of their disease characteristics due to prolonged exposure to other TKIs. Over the range of exposure for 1L participants, the probability of grade \ge 3 lipase increase was low and ranged from 5.8-11.2%, indicating a slight positive relationship between asciminib exposure and probability of grade \ge 3 lipase increase. The probability of occurrence of all other safety events is unlikely to increase with increased exposure of asciminib. Comparable exposure was reached with the 40 mg BID and 80 mg QD asciminib doses, and no association was observed between exposure and most safety events, indicating the interchangeability of these two dosing regimens with respect to their safety profile [Exposure-safety report-Section 9].
- In the newly diagnosed participants with Ph+ CML-CP, a predicted 70% 48-week MMR rate is achieved based on the participant's dosing history in Study J12301, and an enhanced MMR rate of 75% is predicted in uninterrupted dosing situation. Higher MMR rates is achieved when participants maintained uninterrupted dosing; dose interruptions were predicted to lead to lower MMR rates, highlighting the importance of treatment compliance. The asciminib 80 mg QD and 40 mg BID regimens were predicted to result in comparable MMR rates across treatment lines and timepoints. The model-based analysis that characterized the longitudinal BCR::ABL1 by treatment lines (1L, 2L or 3L+) is suitable for extrapolation across lines of asciminib treatment in CML-CP. In the 2L, the total daily dose of 80 mg administered either as once-daily (80 mg QD) or twice-daily (40 mg BID) is predicted to achieve a Week-48 MMR rate of 54-63% and a Week-96 MMR rate of 66-74% [ER-efficacy-report-Section 7].
L1273: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1274: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

The plots compare two treatment groups: Asciminib (solid green line) and Investigator Selected TKI (dashed blue line). Row 3: MR4.5 (FAS, IMA Full Analysis Set, 2G TKI Full Analysis Set)
In all plots, the Asciminib group consistently shows a higher cumulative incidence of the event compared to the Investigator Selected TKI group across the time points shown.

L1313: Box plot showing BCR::ABL (%IS) versus Weeks from baseline (Baseline, Week 4, Week 8, Week 12, Week 24, Week 36, Week 48) for two groups: Asciminib (green boxes) and Investigator Selected TKI (blue boxes). L1314: Figure 7 displays the exposure-efficacy relationship for Study J12301, showing the probability of achieving MMR at Week 48 (top row) and the distribution of AUC_{0-24} and C_{min} quantiles (middle and bottom rows).
The top row shows the Probability of MMR at Week 48 (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus Daily average AUC_{0-24} (ng.h/mL) (X-axis, 5000 to 40000). A logistic regression curve (blue line) and 95% confidence interval (gray shaded area) are shown. Red squares with error bars represent the mean probability of MMR at Week 48 for different AUC_{0-24} levels.
The middle row shows box plots of AUC_{0-24} Quantile (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4) versus Daily average AUC_{0-24} (ng.h/mL). The box plots compare the distribution of AUC_{0-24} for responders (blue boxes) and non-responders (red boxes).
The bottom row shows the distribution of responders (Yes/No) versus Daily average AUC_{0-24} (ng.h/mL). Red dots represent non-responders, and blue dots represent responders.
The bottom row also shows the distribution of C_{min} Quantile (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4) versus Daily average C_{min} (ng/mL). The box plots compare the distribution of C_{min} for responders (blue boxes) and non-responders (red boxes).
The bottom row also shows the distribution of responders (Yes/No) versus Daily average C_{min} (ng/mL). Red dots represent non-responders, and blue dots represent responders.

L1316: Figure 8 displays two panels illustrating the relationship between asciminib exposure and the probability of Major Molecular Response (MMR) at Week 48.
The left panel is a scatter plot showing the Probability of MMR at Week 48 (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus Daily average Cmin (ng/mL) (X-axis, 100 to 1100). Data points are shown for both 40 mg b.i.d. and 80 mg q.d. regimens. A fitted curve and shaded area (representing the 10th to 90th percentiles) suggest that both regimens result in comparable MMR rates across the observed exposure range.
The right panel is a line graph showing Steady-State Concentration (ng/mL) (Y-axis, 0 to 1500) versus Time After Dose (hour) (X-axis, 0 to 25). The graph compares the 40 mg b.i.d. regimen (red line) and the 80 mg q.d. regimen (blue line). The shaded areas represent the 10th to 90th percentiles for each regimen. The median concentration profiles are similar, indicating comparable steady-state exposure.
Source: Figure 5-2 of Applicant Exposure-Efficacy Analysis Report
Source: Figure 7-8 of Applicant's Population Pharmacokinetics Report
